Chapters

Hinglish rewrite with original examples and Python code. Concepts adapted from ai-system-design by Amit Shekhar (Apache-2.0) and ai-system-design-guide by Om Bharatiya (MIT). Not affiliated. Model names, prices and tool details change fast; check current docs.

Chapter 1: LLM, Tokens aur Bill

Phase 0 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 20 minutes

Story: 40 dollar se 4000 dollar

Ek startup ne weekend mein ek support chatbot banaya. Bas ek API key, ek chat() function, aur demo ready. Pehle mahine testing mein bill aaya lagbhag 40 dollar. Sabko laga "ye to bahut sasta hai".

Launch ke baad users aaye, har user ne lambe messages bheje, aur chatbot har baar poori purani conversation dobara bhejta tha. Agle mahine bill 40 se kahin zyada ho gaya aur kisi ko samajh nahi aaya ki kyun.

Jawab ek hi cheez mein tha: tokens.

Problem: hum "words" sochte hain, model "tokens" gina karta hai

  • Hum sochte hain: "Maine 100 words bheje."
  • LLM aur uska bill sochte hain: "Kitne tokens aaye aur kitne tokens gaye?"

Jab tak ye farq clear nahi hota, tab tak na cost samajh aati hai, na latency, na context window ka limit.

Solution: pehle LLM ka kaam samjho

LLM (Large Language Model): ek model jo text padhta hai aur sirf ek kaam karta hai: agla token predict karna.

Token: text ka chhota tukda. Poora word bhi ho sakta hai, word ka hissa bhi, ya sirf ek punctuation.

Chocolate bar ki tarah socho. Poora bar sentence hai. Tod ke jo chhote pieces bante hain, wo tokens hain.

Rough estimate (English ke liye, "usually"): 1 token = lagbhag 4 characters. Chhote common words ("AI", "Hello") aksar 1 token hote hain. Lambe ya rare words kai tokens mein tootte hain.

Hindi ya Hinglish text aksar English se zyada tokens leta hai, lekin ye tokenizer par depend karta hai. Neeche wale code se apne text par khud check karo, andaza mat lagao.

Mental model: autoregressive generation

LLM ek baar mein poora jawab nahi likhta. Wo ek-ek token likhta hai, aur apna hi likha hua token agle step ka input bana leta hai. Isko autoregressive kehte hain.

Input:   "I love"
Step 1:  model sochta hai -> "teaching"      -> "I love teaching"
Step 2:  model sochta hai -> "AI"            -> "I love teaching AI"
Step 3:  model sochta hai -> <stop>

Har step par model ke paas ek token nahi hota, balki har possible token ki probability hoti hai, aur ummeed se ek chun liya jaata hai.

Temperature: ek setting jo is chunav ko control karti hai. Low temperature par model lagbhag hamesha sabse probable token chunta hai (stable). High par zyada random (creative, kam predictable).

Isiliye same prompt do baar bhejo to jawab alag aa sakta hai. Ye AI system design ki sabse badi alag baat hai: normal API deterministic hoti hai, LLM nahi.

Flow: ek request ka bill kaise banta hai

Tum bhejte ho:          [system prompt] + [purani chat history] + [naya sawal]
                        \________________ INPUT tokens _______________/

Model likhta hai:       [jawab, ek-ek token]
                        \____ OUTPUT tokens ____/

Kuch reasoning models:  [hidden "sochne" wale tokens]  -> dikhte nahi, par bill aata hai
                        \____ REASONING tokens ____/

Teen tarah ke tokens:

TypeKya haiCost ka pattern
InputJo tum bhejte hoSasta (parallel mein process hota hai)
OutputJo model likhta haiUsually input se kai guna mehnga (ek-ek karke bante hain)
ReasoningHidden thinking tokens (reasoning models mein)Dikhte nahi, par bill mein aate hain

Chapter 4 mein hum dekhenge ki output tokens mehnge kyun hote hain. Abhi bas ye yaad rakho.

Code: cost aur tokens khud napo

Pehle tokens gino. (pip install tiktoken. Ye OpenAI-style tokenizer hai, doosre models mein count thoda alag hoga; yahan maqsad andaza banana hai.)

import tiktoken

enc = tiktoken.get_encoding("cl100k_base")

for text in ["Hello", "Bangalore", "ChatGPT", "mujhe system design seekhna hai"]:
    ids = enc.encode(text)
    print(f"{text!r}: {len(text)} chars -> {len(ids)} tokens")

Apne Hinglish aur Hindi text ke saath bhi try karo aur dekho.

Ab ek request ka cost:

def estimate_cost(input_tokens: int, output_tokens: int,
                  in_price_per_m: float, out_price_per_m: float) -> float:
    """Prices are per 1 million tokens."""
    return (input_tokens / 1_000_000) * in_price_per_m \
         + (output_tokens / 1_000_000) * out_price_per_m


# Example numbers only. Check your provider's pricing page for real prices.
cost = estimate_cost(1000, 500, in_price_per_m=3.0, out_price_per_m=15.0)
print(f"${cost:.4f}")   # 0.0105  -> about 1 cent

Dhyan do:

  • Prices estimate_cost ke bahar se aati hain, hardcode mat karo. Ye jaldi badalti hain.
  • 1 cent chhota lagta hai. Lekin 1 lakh requests par ye 1000 dollar hai.

Wapas story par: startup ka bill kyun phata?

Chatbot har request mein poori history dobara bhejta tha. Conversation lambi hui to har naye message ke saath input tokens badhte gaye. Matlab ek hi user ka 20th message pehle message se kai guna mehnga tha.

Message 1 :  input =   200 tokens
Message 5 :  input = 1,000 tokens
Message 20:  input = 4,000+ tokens   <- user ko lagta hai "bas ek chhota sawal"

Fixes (aage ke chapters mein detail):

  • History ko chhota karna (truncate ya summarize).
  • Repeating hissa (system prompt) ke liye prompt caching.
  • Har request par tokens aur cost log karna, taaki bill se pehle dikhe.

Production reality

  • Har request par tokens log karo (input, output, cost). Phase 1 project mein tum yahi banaoge.
  • max_tokens hamesha set karo. Warna ek bug ya lambe jawab se bill bahut badh sakta hai.
  • Loop mein LLM call = risk. Ek buggy loop raat bhar chal ke bahut paisa jala sakta hai. Limits aur alerts lagao.
  • Same prompt, alag jawab. Isliye testing sirf assert answer == expected se nahi hoti. Isi liye evals ek poora topic hai (Phase 2).

Trade-offs

  • Zyada context dene se jawab behtar ho sakta hai, lekin cost aur latency badhti hai.
  • Bada model behtar jawab deta hai, lekin mehnga aur slow hota hai.
  • Low temperature stable hai, lekin kam creative.

Common galtiyan

  • "Word count = token count." Nahi hai.
  • "Sirf output ka paisa lagta hai." Nahi. Input bhi bill hota hai, aur long chat history sabse bada hissa ban sakta hai.
  • "Demo mein sasta tha to production mein bhi sasta hoga." Real users lambe aur zyada messages bhejte hain.
  • "Temperature 0 matlab hamesha same jawab." Usually kaafi stable hota hai, lekin guarantee nahi hoti.

Connection

  • Chapter 3 mein hum latency ke naye metrics (TTFT, TPS) dekhenge, jo isi token-by-token generation se nikalte hain.
  • Chapter 4 batata hai ki input tokens sasta aur output tokens mehnga kyun hota hai.
  • Phase 1 project mein tum llm.py mein tokens log karoge. Aaj ka chapter uska theory hai.

Remember

LLM ek-ek token karke likhta hai, aur bill tokens ka aata hai, words ka nahi. Jo tum bhejte ho (history samet) wo bhi bill hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Tumhare chatbot ka bill user ki conversation lambi hone par kyun badhta hai, jabki har naya sawal chhota hai?
  2. Same prompt dobara bhejne par jawab alag kyun aa sakta hai? Production mein tum iske baare mein kya karoge?
  3. Ek request mein 2000 input aur 300 output tokens hain. Input price 3 dollar aur output price 15 dollar per million hai. Cost kitni hogi?

Apne answers mujhse check karwa lo. Pehle khud likho, phir bhejo.

Chapter 2: Inference Server aur Hardware

Phase 0 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 20 minutes

Story: "Hum apna model khud chalayenge, sasta padega"

Ek team ka API bill har mahine badh raha tha. Ek engineer ne kaha: "Open-source Llama download karte hain, apne server par chalate hain. API ka paisa bachega."

Team ne ek normal web server (jaisa wo Node ya FastAPI ke liye use karte the) par model load karne ki koshish ki. Model load hi nahi hua, ya hua to ek jawab dene mein bahut der lagi. Aur jab 10 users ek saath aaye to sab ruk gaya.

Unhe pata chala ki LLM ko chalane ke liye normal server kaafi nahi hota. Isliye ek alag cheez hoti hai: inference server.

Problem: LLM ek normal web app jaisa nahi hai

Normal backend: request aayi, thoda CPU aur database ka kaam hua, response gaya, milliseconds mein.

LLM: lakhon-arabon numbers (model weights) ko memory mein rakhna padta hai, aur har ek token banane ke liye unhe use karna padta hai. Ye kaam normal CPU par bahut slow hota hai aur GPU par fast hota hai.

Aur GPU kam hote hain, mehnge hote hain, aur ek GPU par poori memory ek model aur uski cache mein lagti hai.

Solution: Inference Server

Inference server: ek special server jo model ko GPU memory mein load karta hai aur requests ka jawab deta hai, aur ye kaam smartly karta hai (jaise kai users ki requests ek saath chalana).

Inference: trained model ko use karke jawab nikalna. (Training: model ko sikhana. Inference: sikhe hue model se kaam lena.)

Tum jab OpenAI ya Anthropic ki API call karte ho, tum inference server khud nahi chalate. Tum unka inference server internet par call karte ho.

Jab tum apna model (Llama, Mistral) chalate ho, tab tum inference server (jaise vLLM) GPU machine par chalate ho, aur tumhara backend usko call karta hai.

Mental model: ek mehnga chef

Normal server = ek kitchen jisme kai saste cooks hain. Inference server = ek kitchen jisme ek bahut mehnga chef (GPU) hai.

Aur ye chef ek second ke liye bhi khali baithe to paisa barbaad hota hai. Isliye AI system design ka bada hissa ye hai:

  • GPU ko hamesha busy rakho (kai requests ek saath).
  • Same kaam dobara GPU se mat karwao (caching).
  • Aasan sawaal sasta model sambhale (routing).

Ye teen cheezein aage ke chapters ka roadmap hain.

Flow

Client (browser/app)
        |
        v
Backend server  (tumhara FastAPI / Node code)
        |
        v
Inference server  (GPU machine, model memory mein loaded)
        |
        v
LLM weights + KV cache (GPU memory)

Tumhara backend normal code hai. Sirf "LLM wala dabba" alag aur mehnga hai.

Kaun-kaun se inference engines hote hain (naam yaad rakhne ke liye)

Ye tools jaldi badalte hain, to naam se zyada role yaad rakho.

EngineKab sochna chahiye
vLLMAam default choice, bahut popular open-source option
SGLangMulti-turn chat, structured output, RAG jaisi cheezein jahan prompt ka shuru ka hissa baar-baar repeat hota hai
TensorRT-LLMNVIDIA GPUs par maximum speed, lekin NVIDIA se bandh jaate ho
llama.cpp / OllamaLaptop ya chhoti machine par local chalane ke liye
TGIKabhi common tha, ab maintenance mode mein bataya gaya hai (guide ke hisaab se Dec 2025 ke aas-paas)
Jo bhi tum use karo, current docs check karo. Is table ko "kaun kis kaam ka hai" ki jhalak samjho, aakhri sach nahi.

Hardware: CPU, GPU, TPU, LPU

ChipSeedhi baat
CPUGeneral worker. Kuch bhi kar sakta hai, par ek baar mein thoda
GPUHazaron workers ki team. Parallel math mein bahut tez. AI ke liye default
TPUGoogle ka chip, machine learning math ke liye bana hai
LPUGroq jaisa specialist chip, sirf trained model chalane (tez jawab dene) ke liye

Ek badi baat (yaad rakhne layak): jab LLM jawab likh raha hota hai, tab bottleneck math nahi, memory hoti hai.

Har ek token banane ke liye chip ko model ke poore weights memory se padhne padte hain. To speed is baat par depend karti hai ki data memory se chip tak kitni tezi se aata hai, na ki chip kitna math kar sakta hai.

Isi baat se aage ke chapters ka bahut kuch samajh aayega (prefill vs decode, KV cache).

Code: apne code ko provider se alag rakho

Tum API use karo ya apna inference server, tumhara code ek hi tarah ka rehna chahiye. Bahut se inference servers (vLLM, Ollama) ek OpenAI-compatible API dete hain. Matlab sirf base_url badalna padta hai.

from openai import OpenAI

# Option 1: hosted API
# client = OpenAI(api_key=os.environ["OPENAI_API_KEY"])

# Option 2: tumhara apna local/self-hosted server (vLLM ya Ollama)
client = OpenAI(base_url="http://localhost:11434/v1", api_key="not-needed")

resp = client.chat.completions.create(
    model="llama3.2",                       # jo model tumne local mein pull kiya ho
    messages=[{"role": "user", "content": "Namaste, ek line mein batao inference kya hai"}],
    max_tokens=100,
)
print(resp.choices[0].message.content)

Dhyan do:

  • Port aur model name tumhare setup par depend karte hain. Apne tool ka current doc dekho.
  • Ye wahi idea hai jo Phase 0 mein llm.py wrapper ke liye bataya tha: baki code ko pata hi nahi chalna chahiye ki peeche kaun sa provider hai.

Production reality: "pehle simple, phir scale"

Simple solution (pehle yahi banao)

Hosted API use karo. GPU nahi, ops nahi, scaling provider ka kaam. Zyada tar naye products ke liye ye sahi shuruaat hai.

Production-scale solution (jab zaroorat ho)

Apna inference server tab sochna jab:

  • Cost bahut badh gayi ho aur usage predictable ho.
  • Data kisi bhi haal mein bahar nahi bhej sakte (privacy/compliance).
  • Tumhe custom ya fine-tuned model chalana ho.

Phir bhi, GPU kharidna ya kiraye par lena bahut mehnga ho sakta hai, utilization (GPU kitna busy hai) pe dhyan rakhna padta hai, aur ye ek poora ops ka kaam hai. Ye team ka ek poora role ban jaata hai.

Trade-offs

Hosted APISelf-hosted
ShuruaatAasan, key aur codeMushkil, GPU, setup
CostPay per tokenFixed GPU cost, tab sasta jab busy rakho
ControlKamZyada
Data privacyProvider ke terms par dependTumhare control mein
Ops ka kaamProvider karta haiTumhara

Common galtiyan

  • "Open-source model free hai, to sasta hoga." Model free hai, GPU nahi.
  • Normal web server par model chalane ki koshish.
  • GPU ko idle chhodna. Sabse mehnga resource khali baitha hai.
  • Apna code provider ke SDK se chipka dena. Badalna mushkil ho jaata hai.

Connection

  • Chapter 1 mein tokens aaye. Inference server wahi tokens GPU par ek-ek karke banata hai.
  • Chapter 3 mein hum dekhenge ki iski speed kaise napte hain (TTFT, TPS, throughput).
  • Phase 1 project mein tumhara FastAPI service is inference server (ya hosted API) ke saamne "backend server" wala dabba hai.

Remember

LLM ek mehnge GPU chef par chalta hai jiska asli bottleneck memory hai. Isliye AI system design ka bada hissa us chef ko khali na baithne dene mein jaata hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Normal web server par LLM chalana kyun kaam nahi karta? Inference server alag se kya karta hai?
  2. Ek startup ko apna model self-host karna chahiye ya hosted API se shuru karna chahiye? Kab decision badlega?
  3. "Decode ke time bottleneck memory hai, math nahi" ka matlab apne shabdon mein samjhao.

Chapter 3: TTFT, TPS, Throughput (AI ki speed ke naye metrics)

Phase 0 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 20 minutes

Story: do chatbots, ek hi jawab, alag feel

Do chatbots ek hi lamba jawab dete hain, jisme 10 second lagte hain.

  • Chatbot A: 10 second tak blank screen. Phir poora jawab ek saath.
  • Chatbot B: 0.8 second mein pehla shabd dikhta hai, phir shabd ek-ek karke aate rehte hain, aur 10 second mein jawab poora hota hai.

Dono ka total time same hai. Lekin users ko B "fast" lagta hai, aur A "hang ho gaya" lagta hai.

Normal API ke liye hum sirf ek number napte hain: response time. LLM ke liye ek number kaafi nahi hai.

Problem: ek hi "latency" kaafi kyun nahi?

Kyunki LLM output ek-ek token karke aata hai (Chapter 1). Ek lamba jawab ke andar:

  • Shuru mein ek wait hota hai (jab model prompt padh raha hai).
  • Phir tokens ek ke baad ek aate hain, kisi speed par.

Ye do alag cheezein hain, aur dono alag jagah se kharab ho sakti hain. Isliye AI mein naye metrics aate hain.

Solution: 4 metrics

TTFT (Time To First Token): request bhejne se pehla token aane tak ka time.

Example: "Tell me a story" bheja, 800 ms baad "Once" dikha. TTFT = 800 ms.

TPS (Tokens Per Second): pehle token ke baad model kitni tezi se tokens deta hai.

Example: 50 tokens/sec. To 500 tokens ka jawab lagbhag 10 second mein poora hoga.

TPOT (Time Per Output Token): TPS ka ulta, matlab har token ke beech ka time. 50 TPS = 20 ms per token.

Throughput: server sab users ko milakar kitne tokens per second de raha hai.

Example: 100 users, har ek ko 20 tokens/sec. Throughput = 100 x 20 = 2000 tokens/sec.

Mental model: ek restaurant

RestaurantAI system
Order lene se pehli dish table tak aane ka timeTTFT
Baaki dishes kitni jaldi ek ke baad ek aati hainTPS / TPOT
Poora restaurant ek ghante mein kitni dishes bana raha haiThroughput

Ek chef har table ko jaldi serve kare (low latency), ya ek saath kai tables ko bulk mein (high throughput). Dono ek saath mushkil hai. Yahi trade-off hai.

Flow

Request bheji
   |
   |<------------ TTFT ------------>|
   |                                |
   v                                v
[ model prompt padh raha hai ]   [pehla token]  [tok][tok][tok][tok] ... [done]
                                      |<-- TPOT -->|
                                      (har token ke beech ka gap)

Total time  =  TTFT  +  (output tokens - 1) x TPOT

Example: TTFT 0.8s, 500 tokens, TPOT 20 ms. Total = 0.8 + 499 x 0.02 = about 10.8 second.

Teeno ek-doosre se ladte hain

  • Agar server kai requests ek saath batch kar de, throughput badhta hai (GPU zyada busy rehta hai).
  • Lekin ek single user ke liye TTFT badh sakta hai, kyunki wo doosron ke saath line mein hai.

Matlab "sab kuch best" mushkil hai. Pehle ye tay karo ki tumhare product mein kya zyada zaroori hai:

ProductKya zyada zaroori
Live chat / voiceLow TTFT aur smooth TPS
Raat ko chalne wala bulk summarizationHigh throughput, TTFT ki parwah nahi

Code: apni speed khud mapo

Ye function kisi bhi stream ke liye TTFT aur TPS mapta hai. Neeche ek fake stream hai, to API key ki zaroorat nahi.

import time


def fake_stream(n_tokens=50, ttft=0.8, tpot=0.02):
    """Pretend LLM: first token after `ttft` seconds, then one token every `tpot`."""
    time.sleep(ttft)
    yield "tok"
    for _ in range(n_tokens - 1):
        time.sleep(tpot)
        yield "tok"


def measure(stream):
    start = time.perf_counter()
    first_at = None
    count = 0
    for _ in stream:
        count += 1
        if first_at is None:
            first_at = time.perf_counter() - start
    total = time.perf_counter() - start

    decode_time = total - first_at if first_at is not None else 0.0
    tps = (count - 1) / decode_time if count > 1 and decode_time > 0 else 0.0
    return {"ttft_s": round(first_at or 0.0, 3), "tps": round(tps, 1), "total_s": round(total, 3)}


print(measure(fake_stream()))

Dhyan do:

  • Real API mein ek "chunk" aksar ek token ke barabar hota hai, par hamesha nahi. To ye number andaza hai, exact nahi.
  • Phase 1 project mein tum isi tarah ka measure /chat/stream par lagaoge.

Ek chhota experiment: fake_stream(ttft=0.2) aur fake_stream(tpot=0.1) chalao. Dekho kaunsa number badalta hai, aur socho ki user ko kaunsa zyada khalega.

Production reality

  • Percentiles dekho, average nahi. Average TTFT 1s ho sakta hai, par 5% users ko 8s lag rahe hon. Dashboard mein p50 aur p95/p99 rakho.
  • TTFT aur TPS alag-alag alert karo. Dono ke alag causes hote hain (Chapter 4).
  • Streaming se perceived latency kam hoti hai (Chapter 5), par total time wahi rehta hai.
  • Cost aur latency saath dekho. Sasta aur slow, ya mehnga aur fast, kabhi dono saath nahi milte.

Trade-offs

  • Batching: throughput up, per-user TTFT up.
  • Bada model: better quality, TPS down.
  • Lambi prompt: better context, TTFT up (Chapter 4 mein kyun).

Common galtiyan

  • Sirf total response time napna.
  • Average par chalna aur tail latency (p95/p99) ignore karna.
  • Marketing wale "tokens/sec" number ko apne workload ka number maan lena. Wo aksar kisi aur prompt length aur batch size par napa hota hai.
  • TTFT aur TPS ko ek hi cheez samajhna.

Connection

  • Chapter 1: output ek-ek token karke aata hai, isliye ye metrics bane.
  • Chapter 4: TTFT kyun prompt length par depend karta hai aur TPS kyun memory par, ye wahin samajh aayega.
  • Chapter 5: streaming se TTFT user ko kaise dikhai deta hai.

Remember

TTFT batata hai kitni jaldi shuru hua, TPS batata hai kitni smooth chal raha hai, throughput batata hai kitne logon ko ek saath sambhal sakte ho.

Test Yourself

  1. Do systems ka total response time same (10s) hai, par ek mein TTFT 0.5s aur doosre mein 9s hai. User experience kaise alag hoga?
  2. 200 users ko har ek 15 tokens/sec mil rahe hain. Throughput kitna hai? Agar tum batching badhao to kaunsa metric usually behtar aur kaunsa kharab ho sakta hai?
  3. Tumhare dashboard mein average TTFT 1s dikh raha hai, phir bhi users complain kar rahe hain. Tum kya check karoge?

Chapter 4: Prefill aur Decode (LLM ke andar do phases)

Phase 0 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes

Story: lambi prompt, slow shuruaat

Ek legal-tech product ne apne chatbot mein 50 page ka contract prompt mein daalna shuru kiya. Ab ek ajeeb baat dikhi:

  • Chhota sawal puchho: jawab 0.5 second mein shuru.
  • Contract ke saath puchho: pehla shabd aane mein kai second lagte hain. Lekin jab shuru ho gaya, shabd normal speed se aate hain.

Aur ek aur baat: API ke price page par input tokens sasta aur output tokens mehnga kyun hai?

Dono sawalon ka jawab ek hi jagah hai: LLM har request ko do alag phases mein poora karta hai.

Problem: ek model, do bilkul alag kaam

Chapter 3 mein TTFT aur TPS alag metrics the. Ab sawal ye hai ki ye alag kyun hain, jab model ek hi hai, weights bhi ek hi hain?

Kyunki model do alag tarah ka kaam karta hai, aur dono hardware ko alag tarah se stress karte hain.

Solution: Prefill aur Decode

Prefill: model tumhara poora prompt ek saath padhta hai aur pehla output token banata hai.

Decode: model baaki jawab ek-ek token karke likhta hai.

Mental model: exam hall

  • Prefill = question paper padhna. Student likhne se pehle poora paper chup-chap padhta hai. Saare sawal saamne hain, to ek nazar mein sab padh sakta hai.
  • Decode = answer likhna. Ab word by word likhna padta hai. Pichhla word likhe bina agla nahi aa sakta.

Aur pantry wala chef:

  • Prefill: chef ek bar mein bahut saari sabziyan ek saath kaat raha hai. Haath bahut busy.
  • Decode: chef har baar pantry mein jaata hai, ek sabzi laata hai, kaat ke wapas jaata hai. Kaatne mein thoda time, pantry aane-jaane mein zyada time.

Technical flow

 Prompt (4000 tokens)
        |
        v
 [ PREFILL ]  saare 4000 tokens ek saath process
        |       -> KV Cache bana (har token ke K aur V save)
        |       -> pehla output token
        v
 [ DECODE ]   token 2, token 3, token 4 ... ek-ek karke
        |       -> har step mein poora weights + KV cache memory se padhna
        v
 Jawab poora

KV Cache: model attention ke liye har token ke do vectors (Key aur Value) banata hai. Prefill inhe ek baar nikaal kar store kar deta hai, taaki decode har step par prompt dobara process na kare. Isko aage (caching ke chapters mein) detail se dekhenge.

Dono phases ka farq

PrefillDecode
Kya karta haiPoora prompt processOutput tokens ek-ek
ParallelismSaare tokens ek saathEk token ek step mein
BottleneckCompute (math speed)Memory bandwidth (data moving speed)
GPU utilizationHighLow (single request ke liye)
Kaun sa metricTTFTTPOT / TPS
Cost kis parPrompt lengthHar step par weights + cache padhna

Guide ke hisaab se prefill mein GPU ke math units bahut busy hote hain, aur decode mein ek request ke liye unka chhota hissa hi use hota hai, kyunki zyada time data aane ka intezaar hota hai. Exact percentages hardware aur workload par depend karte hain, to unhe sirf andaza samjho.

Story ka jawab

  1. Contract ke saath TTFT kyun slow? Prefill poora prompt padhta hai. Prompt lambi = prefill lamba = pehla token der se. Isliye TTFT prompt length se badhta hai.
  2. Pehla token aane ke baad speed normal kyun? Decode ka kaam prompt ki length se zyada model ke size aur memory speed par depend karta hai (cache bada hone se thoda asar padta hai, par ye alag topic hai).
  3. Output tokens mehnge kyun? Input tokens parallel mein process hote hain (ek bada step). Output tokens ek-ek karke, har step par memory se bahut data padhna padta hai. Isliye usually output ki per-token price zyada hoti hai. (Providers ki asli pricing aur reasons unki apni hoti hain, ye model hai samajhne ke liye.)

Ek naya problem: prefill aur decode jhagadte hain

Ek hi GPU par ek user ka bahut lamba prompt aaya. Uska prefill ek bada step hai. Jab tak wo chal raha hai, GPU busy hai aur doosre users ke tokens (jo decode mein hain) ruk jaate hain. Unka jawab atakta hai.

Chunked prefill

Lambe prefill ko chhote tukdon (chunks) mein tod do aur beech-beech mein doosron ke decode steps chalne do.

Bina chunking:
  lamba prompt:  [============ EK BADA PREFILL ============]
  doosre users:  tok ....... (ruka hua) ....... tok

Chunking ke saath:
  lamba prompt:  [chunk1][chunk2][chunk3][chunk4][chunk5]
  doosre users:  tok  tok  tok  tok  tok  tok
                      ^ beech mein decode steps

Ek stove wala chef: bada banquet ek bar mein banaya to baaki tables ka khana ruk gaya. Isliye banquet chhote batches mein banao aur beech mein chhoti dishes nikalte raho.

Note: chunking sirf doosron ko rukne se bachata hai. Prefill khud tez nahi hota.

Prefill-Decode Disaggregation (bade scale par)

Bahut bade systems prefill aur decode ko alag GPUs par chalate hain. Ek group sirf prompt padhta hai aur KV cache banata hai. Doosra group wo cache le kar jawab likhta hai. Dono ek-doosre ko disturb nahi karte.

Request -> Router -> Prefill GPUs (prompt padho, cache banao, pehla token)
                          |
                          | KV cache fast network se bhejo
                          v
                     Decode GPUs (token 2, 3, 4 ...)
                          |
                          v
                   user ko stream

Fayda: TTFT aur TPOT alag-alag tune kar sakte ho, aur dono tarah ke GPUs ko alag scale kar sakte ho. Nuksaan: KV cache har request ke liye network par bhejna padta hai, fast interconnect chahiye, aur system complex ho jaata hai.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple (shuru mein): hosted API ya ek single GPU. Chunked prefill agar tumhara inference engine support karta hai to usually default mein ya ek setting mein hota hai.
  • Production scale: bahut users aur lambe prompts ho to disaggregation sochte hain. Chhoti team ko ye shuru mein nahi chahiye. Premature complexity sabse common galti hai.

Production reality (jo tum apne product mein kar sakte ho)

  • Prompt chhota rakho. Har extra token prefill aur cost badhata hai. Kaam ke chunks hi bhejo (RAG ka kaam yahi hai, Phase 2).
  • Lambi prompt = TTFT badhta hai. Isliye streaming UI mein "soch raha hai..." jaisa indicator rakhna sahi hai.
  • Repeat hone wala prompt ka hissa (system prompt, tool schema) baar-baar prefill hota hai. Isko cache karne ka idea aage aayega (prompt caching).

Trade-offs

  • Lambi context dene se quality badh sakti hai, par TTFT aur cost badhte hain.
  • Chunked prefill: doosron ke liye smooth, par lambe prompt wale ka TTFT thoda bad sakta hai (uska kaam tukdon mein bata hua hai).
  • Disaggregation: bade scale par fayda, chhote scale par sirf complexity.

Common galtiyan

  • "Model slow hai" kehna, bina ye dekhe ki TTFT slow hai (prefill) ya TPS slow hai (decode).
  • Prompt mein sab kuch daal dena ("kya pata kaam aa jaye").
  • Sochna ki prefill aur decode alag models hain. Dono ek hi model aur weights hain.
  • Chunked prefill ko "prefill ko tez karne wala" samajhna.

Connection

  • Chapter 3 ke TTFT aur TPS ab explain ho gaye: TTFT ~ prefill, TPS ~ decode.
  • Chapter 2 ki "bottleneck memory hai" wali baat decode ki hi baat thi.
  • Aage KV cache, batching aur speculative decoding sab decode ko behtar banane ke tareeke hain.

Remember

Prefill = model sawal padhta hai (parallel, compute-heavy, TTFT). Decode = model jawab likhta hai (ek-ek token, memory-heavy, TPS). Lambi prompt pehla token slow karti hai, lamba output total time badhata hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Tumhare product mein user ne 30 page ka document diya aur pehla token 6 second mein aaya, par uske baad smooth chala. Kaunsa phase zimmedar hai aur kyun?
  2. Chapter 1 mein output tokens ko input se mehnga bataya tha. Prefill aur decode ke farq se iska ek reason samjhao.
  3. Chunked prefill ek problem solve karta hai. Wo kaunsi problem hai, aur wo kaunsi cheez tez nahi karta?
  4. (Bonus) Disaggregation chhoti team ke liye shuru mein kyun nahi sochna chahiye?

Chapter 5: Streaming (SSE) aur FastAPI

Phase 0 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes | Isi ka code Phase 1 project mein kaam aayega

Story: 30 second ka blank screen

Ek team ne AI report-writer banaya. Jawab 30 second mein poora hota tha. Testing mein sab ne bola "kaam kar raha hai".

Real users ne 5 second baad page refresh karna shuru kar diya, kyunki unhe laga app hang ho gaya. Refresh ka matlab: pichli request waste, nayi request shuru, aur bill double.

Model ko tez nahi kiya ja sakta tha. Lekin ek cheez badal sakti thi: jawab ko ban-te hi bhejna shuru karna.

Problem: normal HTTP "poora ho jaye tab bhejo" wala hota hai

Normal API: server kaam karta hai, kaam khatam hone par ek response bhejta hai.

LLM ka jawab 10 se 30 second le sakta hai. Itni der blank screen par koi nahi rukta.

Solution: Token Streaming

Streaming: server jawab ke tukde (tokens) bante hi client ko bhejta rehta hai. Client use screen par word-by-word dikhata hai.

SSE (Server-Sent Events): ek simple tareeka jisme client ek HTTP connection kholta hai, server use khula rakhta hai, aur beech-beech mein chhote messages likhta rehta hai. Har message data: se shuru hota hai aur ek khali line se khatam. Ant mein ek special marker (jaise [DONE]) bhejte hain.

Mental model: courier vs live commentary

  • Normal HTTP: courier. Poora parcel tayar hone ke baad ek baar mein deliver.
  • Streaming: live cricket commentary. Jaise hi kuch hota hai, turant sunaya jaata hai.

Aur ye samajhna zaroori hai: streaming total time kam nahi karti. Wo sirf pehla shabd jaldi dikha deti hai (TTFT user ko dikhne lagta hai, Chapter 3).

Flow

Normal HTTP

  Client                        Server
    | --- request ------------> |
    |                           | (model 500 tokens banata hai ... 10 sec)
    | <-- poora response ------ |


Streaming (SSE)

  Client                        Server
    | --- request ------------> |
    | <-- "Once"   ------------ |   ~0.8 sec  (TTFT)
    | <-- " upon"  ------------ |
    | <-- " a"     ------------ |
    | <-- " time"  ------------ |
    |         ...               |
    | <-- [DONE]   ------------ |   ~10 sec total

Wire par SSE aisa dikhta hai (plain text):

data: Once

data:  upon

data: [DONE]

Har message ke baad ek khali line zaroori hai. Wahi message ko khatam karti hai.

Code: FastAPI mein SSE endpoint

Ye Phase 0 ke FastAPI module aur Phase 1 project ka seedha hissa hai. Pehle fake tokens se banao, phir real LLM stream se badal dena.

pip install fastapi uvicorn

import asyncio

from fastapi import FastAPI
from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse

app = FastAPI()


async def token_stream(prompt: str):
    # Yahan baad mein real LLM ka stream aayega
    for word in "Once upon a time there was a very slow server".split():
        await asyncio.sleep(0.2)          # LLM ka intezaar pretend karo
        yield f"data: {word}\n\n"        # SSE format: data: ... + khali line
    yield "data: [DONE]\n\n"


@app.get("/chat/stream")
async def chat_stream(prompt: str):
    return StreamingResponse(
        token_stream(prompt),
        media_type="text/event-stream",
        headers={
            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",    # nginx ko buffering band karne ko bolta hai
        },
    )

Chalao: uvicorn main:app --reload

Test karo (terminal mein -N buffering band karta hai, to tokens ek-ek karke dikhenge):

curl -N "http://localhost:8000/chat/stream?prompt=hi"

Samjho line by line:

  • async def + yield: ye async generator hai. Har yield ek tukda bhejta hai, aur await asyncio.sleep ke dauran server baaki requests sambhal sakta hai. (Phase 0 ka async module yahin kaam aata hai.)
  • media_type="text/event-stream": client ko batata hai ki ye SSE hai.
  • X-Accel-Buffering: no: agar beech mein nginx jaisa proxy ho, to wo poora response jama karke ek saath na bheje. Streaming ka sabse common bug yahi hai.

Browser mein (sirf GET request ke liye EventSource kaam karta hai):

const es = new EventSource("/chat/stream?prompt=hi");
es.onmessage = (e) => {
  if (e.data === "[DONE]") { es.close(); return; }
  document.getElementById("out").textContent += e.data + " ";
};

Production reality

Demo mein sab chalta hai. Production mein ye cheezein toot-ti hain:

  • Lambe timeouts. Load balancer ya proxy ka default timeout 30 second ho sakta hai. Streaming connection minutes tak khula reh sakta hai. Timeout badhao.
  • Proxy buffering. Beech ka proxy response ko roke rakhe to user ko sab ek saath milta hai, streaming ka fayda khatam. Buffering band karo (upar header dekha).
  • Client disconnect. User ne tab band kar diya. Agar tumhara server LLM se tokens maangta rahe, to tum unreadable tokens ka paisa de rahe ho. Disconnect detect karke LLM call rok do. FastAPI mein request.is_disconnected() jaisa tareeka hota hai, docs dekho aur Phase 1 mein khud implement karo.
  • Errors beech mein. Stream shuru hone ke baad HTTP status code badla nahi ja sakta (200 chala gaya). Error ko stream ke andar ek special event ke roop mein bhejna padta hai.
  • Retries aur reconnect. Connection beech mein toote to client kya karega? Kya wahin se resume ho sakta hai? Ye design decision hai.
  • Ek connection = ek khula socket. Bahut users ka matlab bahut khule connections. Server ko wo sambhalna aana chahiye (async ka yahi fayda).

Trade-offs: SSE vs WebSocket

SSEWebSocket
DirectionServer se client (ek taraf)Dono taraf
ComplexitySimple, normal HTTPZyada complex
Browser supportEventSource built-in (sirf GET)Built-in, alag protocol
KabChat jahan sirf jawab stream karna haiJab beech mein client se bhi cancel/naya data bhejna ho

Zyada tar LLM chat apps ke liye SSE kaafi hai. Pehle yahi socho.

Common galtiyan

  • "Streaming se jawab jaldi poora ho jaata hai." Nahi, sirf jaldi dikhna shuru hota hai.
  • Khali line (\n\n) bhoolna. Tab client message ko khatam hi nahi maanta.
  • Local par test karna aur production proxy mein buffering ka bug na dekhna.
  • Disconnect handle na karna.
  • Error ko 500 jaisa status code samajhna, jabki stream shuru ho chuki hai.

Connection

  • Chapter 3: streaming user ko TTFT ka fayda dilata hai.
  • Chapter 1: jitne tokens stream hue, utne hi bill mein gaye, chahe user ne padhe ya nahi.
  • Phase 1 project: /chat/stream endpoint isi chapter ka real version hai. Wahan tum fake tokens ki jagah real LLM stream, disconnect handling aur token logging jodoge.

Remember

Streaming ka kaam jawab jaldi poora karna nahi, pehla shabd jaldi dikhana hai. Production mein ye proxy buffering, lambe timeout aur disconnect handling par tikta ya girta hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Streaming se total response time kam hota hai ya sirf kuch aur badalta hai? Kya badalta hai?
  2. Tumhara SSE local par chal raha hai, par production mein user ko sab kuch ek saath aa raha hai. Pehle kya check karoge?
  3. User tab band kar de to server ko kya karna chahiye aur kyun? Isme paise ka kya role hai?
  4. Kab SSE chuno aur kab WebSocket?

Phase 0 ebook yahan khatam

Ab Phase 0 ke practice wale hisse par jao: ../00-foundations.md. Wahan Python revision, FastAPI notes API, aur llm.py banane ka kaam hai. Jab Module C mein streaming aaye to isi chapter ko wapas dekho.

Chapter 6: KV Cache aur Prompt Cache

Phase 1 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes | Pehle Chapter 4 (prefill/decode) padh lena

Story: GPU wahi kaam baar-baar kar raha hai

Ek startup ke support bot ka system prompt 3000 tokens ka hai: rules, tone, policies. Har user message ke saath ye same 3000 tokens jaate hain.

Din bhar mein 1 lakh messages aaye. Matlab GPU ne wahi 3000 tokens 1 lakh baar padhe. Aur jab ek lamba jawab ban raha hota hai, tab model har naye token ke liye pichle saare tokens ka kaam dobara karne baitha tha.

Dono jagah ek hi galti: wahi kaam baar-baar. Iska ilaaj ek hi hai: yaad rakho (cache karo).

Problem 1: ek jawab ke andar (token by token)

Chapter 1 mein dekha ki model ek-ek token likhta hai. Har naye token ke liye attention ko pichhle saare tokens ke Key (K) aur Value (V) chahiye.

Agar K aur V har step par dobara nikaalo:

Step 1: "I love"              -> K,V nikalo: I, love
Step 2: "I love teaching"     -> K,V dobara: I, love  + naya: teaching
Step 3: "I love teaching AI"  -> K,V dobara: I, love, teaching + naya: AI

Pehle ke tokens ka kaam baar-baar ho raha hai. Faltu mehnat.

Solution 1: KV Cache

KV Cache: har token ka K aur V ek baar nikaal kar memory mein rakh lo, aur har agle step mein wahi use karo. Sirf naye token ka K, V nikaalna padta hai.

Meeting ke notes ki tarah: har naye speaker par purane logo se "dobara bolo" nahi kehte, notes padh lete hain, aur sirf naye speaker ko sunte hain.

Guide ka ek example: 100 tokens ke jawab mein bina cache ke K,V ki computation 2+3+...+100 = 5049 hoti hai, cache ke saath lagbhag 101. Matlab lagbhag 50 guna kam kaam, aur sequence jitna lamba, fayda utna zyada.

Sirf K aur V cache karte hain, Query (Q) nahi, kyunki Q sirf current token ko chahiye. Ye "Chapter 4" ki prefill/decode kahani ka hissa hai: prefill cache banata hai, decode use padhta aur badhata hai.

Lekin KV Cache ki apni keemat hai: GPU memory

Cache memory mein tab tak rehta hai jab tak conversation chal rahi hai. Aur wo bada hota hai.

Ek chhota calculation (guide ke Llama-2-jaise model ke numbers: 32 layers, 32 heads, 128 head dimension, har number 2 bytes):

def kv_bytes_per_token(layers, heads, head_dim, bytes_per_number=2):
    # 2 = Key aur Value dono
    return 2 * layers * heads * head_dim * bytes_per_number


per_token = kv_bytes_per_token(layers=32, heads=32, head_dim=128)
print(per_token / (1024 * 1024), "MB per token")          # 0.5
print(4_000 * per_token / (1024 ** 3), "GB for 4k tokens")   # about 1.95
print(100_000 * per_token / (1024 ** 3), "GB for 100k tokens")  # about 48.8

Ye number model ke architecture par depend karta hai (modern models mein GQA jaisi tricks se kam hota hai, Phase aage). Par seekh wahi: cache text ki length aur users ki sankhya ke saath badhta hai, aur GPU memory khaata hai. Isliye GPU par ek saath utne hi lambe conversations chal sakte hain jitna cache sambhal sake.

Isko manage karne ke tareeke (naam yaad rakho):

  • Paged Attention: cache ko chhote fixed-size pages mein bantna, hotel mein ek-ek room dena, na ki pehle se 30 rooms block karna. Memory kam waste hoti hai, zyada users fit hote hain. (vLLM ki famous trick.)
  • KV Cache compression: cache ko chhota karna (kam bits, kam tokens, ya model design se). Quality ke saath thoda trade-off.

Problem 2: alag requests ke beech (same prefix)

Ab wapas startup ke 3000-token system prompt par. Har request mein wo same shuruaat hai, aur har request uska prefill phir se karti hai.

Solution 2: Prompt Cache (prefix cache)

Prompt cache: jab bahut requests ka shuru ka hissa (prefix) bilkul same ho, to us hisse ka KV cache ek baar bana ke reuse karo. Sirf naya hissa (user ka message) naye se process hota hai.

Isliye isko prefix caching bhi kehte hain. Fayda: prefill ka kaam kam, TTFT kam (Chapter 3 aur 4), aur providers mein cached input tokens par aksar discount milta hai (details provider ke docs mein; guide ke hisaab se discount 90% tak ho sakta hai, par ye provider aur model par depend karta hai, pehle check karo).

Teacher board par roz wahi instructions likhta hai. Roz likhne ki jagah board ki photo rakh leta hai.

Sabse zaroori rule: prompt ka order

Cache sirf bilkul same shuruaat par kaam karta hai. Isliye:

  • Jo cheezein fix hain (system prompt, tool definitions, lambe documents) upar rakho.
  • Jo badalti hain (user ka message, aaj ki date) neeche rakho.
# Cache-friendly: fixed upar, badalne wala neeche
messages = [
    {"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM_PROMPT},            # fix, har request mein same
    {"role": "user", "content": f"Question: {user_question}"},  # badalta hai
]

# Cache-unfriendly: shuru mein hi kuch badal raha hai
messages = [
    {"role": "system", "content": f"Today is {now}. {SYSTEM_PROMPT}"},  # timestamp upar!
    {"role": "user", "content": user_question},
]

Doosre example mein har request ka prefix alag ho gaya (timestamp badal gaya), to prefix cache kabhi hit nahi hoga. Ye bahut common aur silent galti hai.

Kuch providers mein prompt caching ke liye minimum prefix length ya explicit setting chahiye hoti hai. Apne provider ka current doc dekho.

Flow

Request 1:  [ SYSTEM PROMPT 3000 tok ][ user msg A ]
                 | prefill poora, KV cache save
                 v
            cache: {prefix -> KV}

Request 2:  [ SYSTEM PROMPT 3000 tok ][ user msg B ]
                 | prefix cache HIT  -> 3000 tokens ka prefill skip
                 v
            sirf "user msg B" ka prefill  -> TTFT kam, cost kam

Production reality

  • Prefix stable rakho. System prompt mein har request pe kuch badalna (timestamp, user name, random id) cache todta hai. Aise cheezein neeche le jao.
  • Cache hit rate napo. Providers aksar response mein batate hain ki kitne tokens cache se aaye. Usse log karo. Hit rate gira to kisi ne prompt ka order bigaad diya.
  • Cache ki umar hoti hai. Provider ka cache kuch der baad expire ho sakta hai. Rare traffic wale prompts ke liye fayda kam.
  • Self-hosted engine: vLLM aur SGLang jaise engines prefix caching support karte hain. SGLang ko aksar RAG jaise prefix-heavy kaam mein mana jaata hai.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: hosted API use karo, prompt ka order sahi rakho, aur cached tokens ka number log karo. Bas itna kaafi fayda deta hai.
  • Production-scale: self-host karte ho to paged attention, prefix-aware routing (same prefix wali requests ek hi GPU par bhejna), cache compression sochte ho.

Trade-offs

  • KV cache: speed ke liye memory dena. Lambi contexts = bahut GPU memory.
  • Prompt cache: fayda tab jab prefix bahut repeat ho. Agar har request ka prompt alag hai to kuch nahi milta.
  • Compression: memory bachti hai, par quality par thoda asar ho sakta hai.

Common galtiyan

  • "KV cache aur prompt cache ek hi cheez hain." Dono ka base (K, V store karna) same hai, par KV cache ek request ke andar kaam karta hai, prompt cache alag requests ke beech.
  • System prompt mein timestamp ya user-specific cheez upar daal dena.
  • Prompt caching ko bina napne "chal raha hoga" maan lena.
  • Lambi context ko free samajhna. Wo cache memory aur GPU kharch karti hai.

Connection

  • Chapter 4: prefill K,V banata hai, decode use padhta hai. Ye chapter usi ka cache wala hissa hai.
  • Chapter 3: prompt cache TTFT kam karta hai.
  • Chapter 1: cached input tokens ki price alag ho sakti hai, to bill par seedha asar.
  • Phase 1 project: system prompt aur tool schemas ko stable aur upar rakho, aur cached-token count /usage mein dikhao.

Remember

KV cache ek jawab ke andar purana kaam dobara hone se bachata hai. Prompt cache alag requests ke beech same shuruaat ka kaam dobara hone se bachata hai. Dono ke liye: jo badalta nahi wo upar rakho.

Test Yourself

  1. KV cache mein Query (Q) store kyun nahi karte, sirf K aur V?
  2. Tumhare system prompt mein "Current time: ..." sabse upar likha hai aur prompt cache hit rate zero hai. Kyun? Fix kya hai?
  3. KV cache ki wajah se ek GPU par bahut saare lambe conversations ek saath kyun nahi chal sakte?
  4. KV cache aur prompt cache mein ek farq batao.

Chapter 7: Response Cache, Semantic Cache aur LLM Routing

Phase 1 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes

Story: "France ki capital kya hai?" 5000 baar

Ek education app mein roz hazaron students same sawal puchte hain: "France ki capital?", "Capital city of France?", "tell me capital of france". Har baar LLM call hoti hai, har baar paisa lagta hai, aur har baar 2 second wait.

Doosri taraf, "hi" aur "thanks" jaise messages bhi sabse mehnge model par ja rahe hain.

Do sawal uthte hain:

  1. Jo sawal pehle poochha ja chuka hai, uska jawab dobara LLM se kyun banwayein?
  2. Aasan sawal ke liye mehnga model kyun?

Problem: bahut LLM calls bekaar jaa rahi hain

Chapter 6 ke caches GPU ke andar ka kaam bachate the. Ab hum poori LLM call hi bachana chahte hain.

Solution 1: Exact-match response cache

Sabse simple: agar bilkul same request pehle aa chuki hai, to saved jawab de do.

request -> cache key banao -> cache mein hai? -> haan: jawab do (LLM call nahi)
                                              -> nahi: LLM call, jawab save, jawab do

Asli sawal: cache key mein kya jaye? Jo bhi jawab ko badal sakta hai, wo key mein hona chahiye.

import hashlib
import json


def cache_key(model: str, messages: list, temperature: float, tenant_id: str) -> str:
    payload = {
        "model": model,              # model badla to jawab badal sakta hai
        "messages": messages,        # poora prompt
        "temperature": temperature,  # settings bhi jawab badalti hain
        "tenant": tenant_id,         # alag users ko ek-doosre ka jawab kabhi na mile
    }
    raw = json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False)
    return "llm:" + hashlib.sha256(raw.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


k1 = cache_key("m1", [{"role": "user", "content": "hi"}], 0.0, "tenantA")
k2 = cache_key("m1", [{"role": "user", "content": "hi"}], 0.0, "tenantB")
print(k1 != k2)   # True: alag tenant, alag key

Phase 1 project mein tum isi ko Redis ke saath TTL laga ke banaoge.

Dhyan do:

  • Temperature ke saath soch kar cache karo. Agar tumne creative (high temperature) jawab maanga, to cache kiya hua ek hi jawab sabko dena product ke liye galat ho sakta hai. Zyada tar factual/stable kaam ke liye cache karo.
  • TTL lagao (jawab kitni der valid hai). Jo jawab badalte rehte hain unka TTL chhota.

Solution 2: Semantic cache

Exact cache "France ki capital?" aur "Capital city of France?" ko alag maanta hai, kyunki strings alag hain. Par matlab same hai.

Embedding: text ko numbers ki list (vector) mein badalna, aisa ki milte-julte matlab ke text ke vectors paas-paas hon.

Semantic cache: sawal ka embedding banao, cache mein sabse milta-julta purana sawal dhundo, aur agar kaafi paas hai to uska saved jawab de do.

import math


def cosine(a, b):
    dot = sum(x * y for x, y in zip(a, b))
    na = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in a))
    nb = math.sqrt(sum(y * y for y in b))
    return dot / (na * nb)


# Fake 3-number "embeddings" sirf samajhne ke liye (real wale 100s-1000s numbers ke hote hain)
q_france      = [0.90, 0.10, 0.00]   # "capital of France?"
q_france_alt  = [0.88, 0.12, 0.02]   # "tell me the capital city of France"
q_germany     = [0.60, 0.70, 0.10]   # "capital of Germany?"

THRESHOLD = 0.95
print(cosine(q_france, q_france_alt) >= THRESHOLD)   # True  -> cache hit
print(cosine(q_france, q_germany) >= THRESHOLD)      # False -> cache miss

Sabse khatarnak setting: similarity threshold

  • Bahut high (jaise 0.99): lagbhag kuch match nahi hota. Fayda gaya.
  • Bahut low (jaise 0.50): "Capital of France" aur "Capital of Germany" match ho sakte hain aur galat shehar jawab mein jata hai.
  • Guide ke hisaab se 0.85 se 0.95 kai apps mein theek rehta hai, par sahi value tumhare real sawalon par test karke nikalni padti hai.
High threshold = kam matches, zyada safe. Low threshold = zyada matches, galat jawab ka risk.

Semantic cache kab mat use karo

  • Jahan jawab user ke private data par depend karta hai ("mera balance kitna hai?"). Kisi aur user ke cached jawab ka milna data leak hai. Aise sawal ke liye semantic cache band, ya tenant-wise alag.
  • Jahan thoda sa farq bhi matlab badal deta hai (numbers, negation, dates). "Refund 7 din mein?" vs "Refund 70 din mein?" bahut paas ke vectors ho sakte hain.
  • Jahan jawab tezi se badalta hai.

Embedding cache (chhota bhai)

RAG mein bahut text ke embeddings banane padte hain, aur embedding banana bhi mehnga kaam hai. Same text ka embedding dobara na banao: text (ya uska hash) ko key aur vector ko value rakho.

Key mein embedding model ka naam aur version zaroor rakho. Alag models alag vectors dete hain, mix karne se search chupke se kharab ho jaati hai.

Solution 3: LLM routing

Har sawal ke liye sabse bada model chalana "har chhote kaam ke liye truck bulana" jaisa hai.

LLM routing: har request ke liye sahi model chunna.

                +-------------+
user query ---> |   Router    |
                +-------------+
                  |     |     |
            small   mid   big model
           (sasta) (beech) (mehnga, accurate)

Router ke tareeke (sabse simple se complex):

  • Rule-based: length, keywords, ya feature ke hisaab se ("greeting" -> chhota model).
  • Classifier-based: ek chhota model/classifier sawal ko "easy/hard" mein baantta hai.
  • Cascade: pehle sasta model try karo, jawab shaky ho (ya check fail ho) to mehnge par bhejo.

Simple rule-based router ka idea:

def pick_model(user_message: str) -> str:
    text = user_message.strip().lower()
    if len(text) < 20 and text in {"hi", "hello", "thanks", "ok"}:
        return "small-model"
    if len(text) > 2000 or "step by step" in text:
        return "big-model"
    return "mid-model"

Ye jaanbujh kar simple hai. Pehle isse shuru karo, aur eval se napo ki kitne jawab kharab hue, phir behtar router sochna.

Production reality

  • Cache hit rate aur "galat hit" dono napo. Hit rate badhne se khush mat ho; sample check karo ki cached jawab sach mein sahi the.
  • Invalidation: jab tumhare data, prompt ya model version badle, to purane cached jawab hata do (key mein version rakhna madad karta hai).
  • Multi-tenant safety: cache key aur semantic search dono mein tenant alag rakho.
  • Routing ka asli test: cost kitni bachi aur quality kitni giri. Dono number chahiye.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: exact-match cache (Redis + TTL) aur ek rule-based router. Aksar yahi kaafi hota hai.
  • Production-scale: semantic cache (vector search ke saath), classifier ya cascade routing, aur eval se napa hua threshold.

Trade-offs

  • Cache: paisa aur latency bachti hai, par stale ya galat jawab ka risk.
  • Semantic cache: zyada hit, par "lagbhag same" ka matlab "bilkul same" nahi.
  • Routing: sasta, par galat router kharab jawab ya extra latency de sakta hai.

Common galtiyan

  • Cache key mein tenant ya model na daalna.
  • Private sawalon par semantic cache.
  • Threshold andaza se rakhna.
  • High-temperature creative jawab ko sabko ek hi dena.
  • Router banana par uski quality napna hi nahi.

Connection

  • Chapter 6: wo GPU-level caches the, ye application-level cache hai (poori call bachana).
  • Chapter 1: har bachi hui call seedha bill kam karti hai.
  • Phase 2 (RAG): embedding cache aur semantic search wahin dobara milenge.
  • Phase 1 project: exact-match Redis cache aur ek simple router yahin se.

Remember

Sabse sasti LLM call wo hai jo hui hi nahi. Pehle same sawal cache se, phir aasan sawal sasta model se. Lekin cache galat ya kisi aur ka jawab na de, ye pakka karo.

Test Yourself

  1. Exact-match cache key mein tum kya-kya daaloge aur har ek kyun?
  2. Semantic cache mein threshold 0.5 rakh diya. Kaunsi problem aayegi? Aur 0.99 par?
  3. "Mera account balance kitna hai?" par semantic cache kyun khatarnak hai?
  4. Cascade routing mein tum kaise decide karoge ki sasta model ka jawab kaafi hai ya mehnge par bhejna hai?

Chapter 8: Rate Limiting for AI

Phase 1 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 20 minutes

Story: ek client ka bug aur raat bhar ka bill

Ek SaaS company ne apna AI feature ek partner ko API ke roop mein diya. Partner ke code mein ek bug tha: failed request par wo bina ruke turant retry karta tha, aur har retry mein poori lambi history bhejta tha.

Raat ko kisi ne dekha nahi. Subah tak lakhon requests, bahut saare tokens, aur company ka mahine ka AI budget kuch ghanton mein khatam.

Company ke paas rate limit tha: "100 requests per minute". Phir bhi ye hua. Kyun?

Problem: AI mein "request" ek barabar nahi hoti

Normal API mein 100 requests/min ek achchi limit hai, kyunki har request lagbhag barabar kaam karti hai.

AI mein:

  • Ek request 100 tokens ki ho sakti hai.
  • Doosri 100,000 tokens ki.

Dono "1 request" hain, par cost aur GPU load hazaron guna alag. Sirf request count se limit lagana aisa hai jaise courier company parcel ka wazan dekhe bina sirf parcel ginti pe charge kare.

Solution: multi-dimensional rate limiting

Ek nahi, kai cheezein saath limit karo.

LimitKya napta haiKya rokta hai
RPM (Requests Per Minute)Kitni requestsRequest spam
TPM (Tokens Per Minute)Kitne tokensBade prompts, history ka dhher
Concurrent requestsEk saath chal rahi requestsEk user ka GPU/connections hog karna (LLM calls lambi chalti hain, isliye ye zaroori)
Cost-basedDollar mein kharchBudget ke bahar jana (daily/monthly cap)

Chapter 1 ka seekha yahan kaam aata hai: bill tokens ka hota hai, to limit bhi tokens par honi chahiye.

Mental model: theatre ki entry

  • RPM: ek minute mein kitne log gate se andar aaye.
  • TPM: un logon ka kul wazan/saman kitna tha.
  • Concurrent: hall ke andar ek saath kitne log baithe hain.
  • Cost cap: poore show ka budget.

Sirf gate par logon ki ginti rakhne se, agar ek aadmi poora truck saman lekar aa jaye, hall bhar jata hai.

Algorithm: token bucket (sabse common idea)

Token bucket: ek balti jisme "permission tokens" hain (ye LLM tokens se alag cheez hai, naam ek jaisa hai). Balti dheere-dheere bharti rehti hai. Har request balti se kuch nikalti hai. Balti khali to request ruk jaati hai.

Isme burst (achanak thodi zyada traffic) bhi chal jaati hai, par lambe samay ka average limit mein rehta hai.

import time


class TokenBucket:
    def __init__(self, capacity: float, refill_per_sec: float):
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.refill_per_sec = refill_per_sec
        self.tokens = capacity
        self.updated = time.monotonic()

    def allow(self, cost: float = 1.0) -> bool:
        now = time.monotonic()
        self.tokens = min(self.capacity, self.tokens + (now - self.updated) * self.refill_per_sec)
        self.updated = now
        if self.tokens >= cost:
            self.tokens -= cost
            return True
        return False


# 10,000 LLM tokens per minute per user
bucket = TokenBucket(capacity=10_000, refill_per_sec=10_000 / 60)
print(bucket.allow(cost=6_000))   # True
print(bucket.allow(cost=6_000))   # False (balti mein sirf 4000 bache)

Is chhote code se do seekh:

  • cost request ke size (tokens) ka hota hai, 1 nahi. Yahi AI aur normal rate limit ka farq hai.
  • Alag user ke liye alag bucket.

Ek tedhi baat: output ka size pehle se pata nahi hota

Input tokens request aate hi pata hain. Output kitna aayega, ye jawab ban-ne ke baad pata chalta hai. To usually:

  1. Request aate hi andaza (input tokens + max_tokens) se balti mein se kaat lo.
  2. Jawab aane ke baad asli use dekh kar difference wapas karo ya aur kaato.

Isliye hamesha max_tokens set karna zaroori hai (Chapter 1): wahi tumhara worst case hai.

Flow

Request aayi
    |
    v
user/tenant pehchano (API key)
    |
    v
RPM ok?  TPM ok?  Concurrent ok?  Cost cap ok?
    | (koi bhi fail)           | (sab pass)
    v                          v
HTTP 429 + Retry-After     LLM ko bhejo -> jawab -> asli tokens se hisaab theek karo

Fail hone par 429 Too Many Requests aur Retry-After header do, taaki client ko pata chale kitni der ruke.

Production reality

  • Multiple servers ho to counter shared chahiye. In-memory bucket sirf ek process mein kaam karta hai. 3 containers ho to har ek alag gin raha hai. Isliye shared store (Redis) mein counter rakhte hain, aur check-aur-kaato ek hi atomic step mein hona chahiye (warna race condition). Redis ke atomic commands ya Lua script is kaam ke liye use hote hain; apne tool ka doc dekho.
  • Limit kiske liye? Per user, per API key, per tenant, per feature. Ek hi limit sab par lagana aksar galat hota hai (ek bada customer baaki sabko rok dega).
  • Cost cap hard stop hona chahiye, sirf alert nahi. Alert dekhne tak bill ban chuka hota hai.
  • Client ko saaf jawab do: 429, Retry-After, aur kaun si limit hit hui.
  • Story ke bug ka ilaaj: TPM + concurrent limit + daily cost cap. Aur client side par retry mein backoff (Chapter 10).
  • Provider ke limits bhi hote hain. OpenAI/Anthropic ki apni RPM/TPM limits hain. Tumhari limits unse neeche rakho, aur 429 aaye to sambhalna seekho.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: ek process ke liye in-memory limiter, per API key, aur max_tokens ke saath. Phase 1 project mein yahi se shuru karo.
  • Production-scale: Redis-based shared limits, per-tenant plans, daily/monthly cost caps, aur AI gateway mein centralized (agla chapter).

Trade-offs

  • Strict limits = safe budget, par kabhi-kabhi valid users bhi rok diye jaate hain.
  • Andaze se pehle kaatna aur baad mein theek karna: simple, par thoda inexact.
  • Concurrent limit user ko rokta hai par GPU/connections bachata hai.

Common galtiyan

  • Sirf requests/minute par limit.
  • max_tokens set na karna, to worst case ka pata hi nahi.
  • In-memory counter ko multi-server setup mein chalana.
  • Sirf alert lagana, hard cost cap nahi.
  • Rate limit hone par client ko kuch na batana.

Connection

  • Chapter 1: bill tokens ka hota hai, isliye TPM.
  • Chapter 5: streaming mein connection lamba chalta hai, isliye concurrent limit zaroori.
  • Chapter 9: ye saari limits AI gateway mein ek jagah rakhte hain.
  • Chapter 10: limit aur retry ek-doosre se jude hain (429 par retry kaise karna).
  • Phase 1 project: /chat par rate limit aur /usage par cost.

Remember

AI mein request barabar nahi hoti, isliye request ki ginti nahi, tokens aur rupaye par limit lagao. Aur worst case ke liye max_tokens aur hard cost cap rakho.

Test Yourself

  1. Sirf "100 requests/min" ki limit kyun kaafi nahi thi story mein?
  2. Output tokens pehle se nahi pata, to TPM limit kaise lagaoge?
  3. Tumne 3 container chalaye aur har ek mein in-memory limiter hai. Kya galat hoga?
  4. Concurrent request limit AI mein khaas taur par kyun zaroori hai?

Chapter 9: AI Gateway

Phase 1 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 20 minutes

Story: sabki apni LLM call, sabka apna bug

Ek company mein 6 teams ne apne-apne AI features banaye. Har team ne apni API key, apna retry code, apna logging, aur apna cost-tracking likha.

Ek din OpenAI mein outage aaya. Teen teams ka feature band ho gaya, kyunki unhone fallback likha hi nahi tha. Ek team ki key leak ho gayi. Aur finance ne poocha "AI par kul kitna kharch hua, kis feature par?" aur kisi ke paas jawab nahi tha.

Har team wahi cheezein baar-baar bana rahi thi, aur har baar thodi alag aur thodi adhoori.

Problem: LLM ke aas-paas ki zaroori cheezein bikhri hui hain

Pichhle chapters mein hamne kai cheezein dekhi: caching, rate limiting, retries, fallback, cost tracking, logging. Agar har service ye sab alag-alag banaye to:

  • Code repeat hota hai.
  • Rules alag-alag lagte hain (koi limit lagata hai, koi nahi).
  • Kuch change karna ho (provider badalna) to har jagah badalna padta hai.
  • Poori company ki tasveer kahin nahi dikhti.

Solution: AI Gateway

AI Gateway: tumhari apps aur LLM providers ke beech ek single layer jahan se har LLM call guzarti hai. Ye ek reverse proxy hai jo tokens, prompts, streaming aur cost samajhta hai.

Guide ke hisaab se isko aksar infra ke pehle tukdon mein banane ki salaah di jaati hai, kyunki baaki sab concerns iske baad aasan ho jaate hain.

Mental model: airport ka immigration counter

Har passenger (request) ek hi counter se guzarta hai. Wahan passport check (auth), line ka niyam (rate limit), record (logging), aur kisi gate par bheja jaata hai (routing). Har airline apna alag counter nahi banati.

Flow

+----------+        +---------------+        +--------------------+
|  App 1   | -----> |               | -----> | OpenAI             |
|  App 2   | -----> |  AI GATEWAY   | -----> | Anthropic          |
|  App 3   | -----> |               | -----> | Google             |
+----------+        |  auth         | -----> | Self-hosted vLLM   |
                    |  rate limit   |        +--------------------+
                    |  cache        |
                    |  routing      |
                    |  fallback     |
                    |  guardrails   |
                    |  logging/cost |
                    +---------------+

Gateway kya-kya deta hai

FaydaMatlab
Ek endpoint, kai modelsApp ek hi tarah ki call karti hai; provider badalna config change hai
FailoverEk provider down to doosre par chale jao, app ko pata bhi na chale
Cost trackingHar call par user, team, feature, request id ka tag, dashboard aasan
Rate limits aur quotasSab ek jagah (Chapter 8)
CachingPrompt aur semantic cache ek jagah (Chapter 7)
GuardrailsInput/output safety rules company-wide ek jagah
Audit logsDebugging aur compliance ke liye record (par private data ka dhyan, niche dekho)
ObservabilityLatency, tokens, errors, cache hit rate ek dashboard

Popular tools (naam jaanne ke liye; ye jaldi badalte hain): LiteLLM, Portkey, Kong AI Gateway, Envoy AI Gateway, aur cloud providers ke apne gateway jaise services.

Code: gateway ka idea, bahut chhota roop

Poora gateway banane ki zaroorat nahi. Idea samajhne ke liye ek chhota model: alias -> provider list (fallback order ke saath).

# Apps sirf alias bolti hain. Asli model/provider yahan config mein hai.
MODEL_ROUTES = {
    "chat-default": [
        {"provider": "provider_a", "model": "model-a-small"},
        {"provider": "provider_b", "model": "model-b-small"},   # fallback
    ],
    "chat-smart": [
        {"provider": "provider_a", "model": "model-a-large"},
        {"provider": "provider_b", "model": "model-b-large"},
    ],
}


def resolve(alias: str):
    try:
        return MODEL_ROUTES[alias]
    except KeyError:
        raise ValueError(f"unknown model alias: {alias}")


for route in resolve("chat-default"):
    print("will try:", route["provider"], route["model"])

Fayda: kal tumhein "chat-default" ko doosre model par badalna hai to sirf is config mein badlo, kisi app ka code nahi. Ye wahi llm.py wrapper ka bada roop hai jo tumne Phase 0 mein socha tha.

Zyada tar log shuru mein khud ye nahi banate; LiteLLM jaisa tool use karte hain. Par ye idea samajhna zaroori hai taaki tum tool ko samajh sako.

Gateway banate waqt kya galat ho sakta hai

  • Streaming buffer karna. Agar gateway poora jawab jama karke bheje, to Chapter 5 ka SSE ka poora fayda khatam. Gateway ko stream pass-through karna chahiye. Token count aakhri event se log hota hai.
  • Gateway khud slow hona. Har call par overhead jodta hai. Guide ke hisaab se 10 ms ke andar rakhne ka target hota hai.
  • Gateway single point of failure ban jana. Wo gir gaya to sab band. Isliye health checks, kai instances, aur zaroorat par seedha provider ko call karne ka fallback.
  • Logs mein private data. Poore prompts aur responses log karna compliance ka risk hai (PII). Kya log karna hai, kab mask karna hai, kitne din rakhna hai, ye decide karo.
  • Ek hi key ka bharosa. Provider API keys sirf gateway ke paas rakho, apps ke paas nahi. Ek jagah rotate karna aasan hota hai.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple (shuru mein): ek hi service hai to alag gateway ki zaroorat nahi. Ek llm.py wrapper jisme timeout, retry, aur token logging ho, kaafi hai. Phase 1 mein yahi banega.
  • Production-scale: kai teams aur kai providers ho jayein to gateway (LiteLLM jaise tool ya apna) lagao: central keys, limits, cost tracking, failover.

Matlab "har project mein gateway chahiye" aisa nahi hai. Jab duplicate code aur bikhri rules dikhne lagein, tab sochna.

Trade-offs

  • Ek jagah control aur visibility, par ek aur system chalane aur maintain karne ka kaam.
  • Gateway par bharosa: wo gira to sab giren; usko highly available banana padta hai.
  • Extra hop: thoda latency overhead.

Common galtiyan

  • Har team ka apna LLM client aur apni keys.
  • Gateway mein response buffer kar dena aur streaming kharab kar dena.
  • Gateway ko bina fallback ke single point of failure bana dena.
  • Prompts aur responses bina soche log karna.
  • Chhote project mein pehle din se bada gateway lagana (over-engineering).

Connection

  • Chapter 7 (cache, routing), Chapter 8 (rate limits), Chapter 10 (retries, fallback) sab gateway ke andar ek jagah mil jaate hain. Ye chapter unka "ghar" hai.
  • Chapter 5: gateway ko streaming pass-through karna hota hai.
  • Phase 3 mein MCP tools aur Phase 4 mein guardrails bhi aise hi central policy ke roop mein aa sakte hain.
  • Phase 1 project: tumhara llm.py ek mini-gateway hai.

Remember

AI Gateway ek immigration counter hai: har LLM call ek hi jagah se guzarti hai, to limits, cache, fallback, cost aur safety ek hi jagah lagte hain. Par chhote project mein pehle ek acha llm.py kaafi hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Story mein 6 teams ke alag-alag LLM code se kaun-kaun si problems aayi? Gateway inme se kaunsi hal karta hai?
  2. Gateway buffering kyun streaming kharab kar deti hai (Chapter 5 se jodo)?
  3. Ek chhote startup ko pehle din gateway lagana chahiye ya nahi? Kab decision badlega?
  4. Gateway mein poore prompts log karne ka kya risk hai?

Chapter 10: Fault Tolerance (jab sab kuch toote)

Phase 1 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes

Story: provider ka 20 minute ka outage

Ek travel app ka AI assistant din bhar chal raha tha. Shaam ko LLM provider mein 20 minute ka outage aaya.

Assistant ne kuch aise react kiya:

  • Har request 60 second tak latki rahi (koi timeout nahi tha), phir error.
  • Users ne dubara-dubara click kiya, to requests ka dher lag gaya.
  • App ke code ne retry kiya, wo bhi bina ruke, 3 baar turant. Provider par aur load.
  • Jab jawab aaya, kabhi-kabhi wo broken JSON tha aur app crash ho gaya.

Provider ka outage tumhare haath mein nahi tha. App ka crash tumhare haath mein tha.

Problem: AI system ke fail hone ke naye tareeke

Normal systems ki tarah timeout aur server down to hote hi hain. AI mein aur bhi:

  • Provider outage ya rate limit (429).
  • GPU memory khatam hona beech mein.
  • Model ka jawab expected format mein na aana (broken JSON).
  • Vector DB slow hona.
  • Agent loop ka ghoomte rehna ya timeout ho jana.

Solution: 5 cheezein

TechniqueKaam
TimeoutHar call par time limit; der hui to kaat do
Retry with backoffTemporary errors par ruk-ruk kar dobara koshish
Fallback modelPrimary down to doosra model/provider
Graceful degradationAI feature fail ho to app simple experience par aa jaye, crash nahi
Output validationLLM ka jawab use karne se pehle check karo

1. Timeout

LLM call lambi chalti hai, par "bahut lambi" ka koi ant hona chahiye. Streaming mein bhi: pehla token kitni der mein aana chahiye, aur total kitna.

import asyncio


async def call_with_timeout(coro, seconds: float):
    try:
        return await asyncio.wait_for(coro, timeout=seconds)
    except asyncio.TimeoutError:
        raise TimeoutError(f"LLM call exceeded {seconds}s")

Real HTTP clients (jaise httpx) mein bhi timeout setting hoti hai; apne client ka doc dekho.

2. Retry with exponential backoff (aur jitter)

Exponential backoff: har failure ke baad rukne ka time double karo (1s, 2s, 4s...), taaki jo service pehle se mushkil mein hai, use aur na dabao.

Jitter: thoda random time jodo, taaki 1000 clients ek hi second par dobara na ghuse.

import random
import time


def retry(fn, max_attempts=4, base_delay=0.5, retry_on=(TimeoutError, ConnectionError)):
    for attempt in range(1, max_attempts + 1):
        try:
            return fn()
        except retry_on:
            if attempt == max_attempts:
                raise
            delay = base_delay * (2 ** (attempt - 1))
            delay += random.uniform(0, delay * 0.2)     # jitter
            time.sleep(delay)


# Test: do baar fail, teesri baar success
calls = {"n": 0}

def flaky():
    calls["n"] += 1
    if calls["n"] < 3:
        raise ConnectionError("temporary")
    return "ok"

print(retry(flaky, base_delay=0.01), "after", calls["n"], "calls")   # ok after 3 calls

Kin errors par retry karo?

  • Haan: timeout, connection error, 429 (limit), 5xx (server error).
  • Nahi: 400 jaise galat request (bar-bar bhejne se theek nahi hoga), authentication fail.

Bahut zaroori warning: retry sirf safe (idempotent) kaam par karo. "Email bhejo" tool ko timeout par retry kiya aur pehla actually chala gaya tha, to email do baar gaya. Side-effect wale kaam par retry se pehle soch lo (Phase 3 mein dobara aayega: idempotency key).

3. Fallback model

Primary fail ho (retries ke baad bhi), to doosre model ya provider par jao. Gateway (Chapter 9) ye ek jagah kar sakta hai.

def call_with_fallback(routes, call_fn):
    last_error = None
    for route in routes:
        try:
            return call_fn(route)
        except Exception as e:      # production mein sirf expected errors pakdo
            last_error = e
    raise RuntimeError("all providers failed") from last_error

Dhyan do: fallback model ka jawab alag style ka ho sakta hai. Apne prompts aur evals dono par test karo, sirf "kaam karta hai" kaafi nahi.

4. Graceful degradation

AI feature fail ho gaya, to poori app mat girao. Behtar experience socho:

  • Chat assistant down: "AI abhi available nahi hai, support ko contact karein" aur normal search/FAQ chalu.
  • Summary feature down: original text dikhao.
  • Semantic cache ya reranker down: bina uske simple search chalao.

Rule: pehle sochkar rakho ki AI ke bina bhi app ka core kaam karega ya nahi.

5. Output validation

LLM ka jawab bharosemand input nahi, use untrusted input samjho. Jab tumne JSON maanga:

from pydantic import BaseModel, ValidationError


class Ticket(BaseModel):
    category: str
    priority: int


def parse_ticket(raw: str) -> Ticket:
    return Ticket.model_validate_json(raw)


def get_ticket(ask_llm, max_attempts=2):
    prompt = "Return JSON with keys category (str) and priority (int)."
    for attempt in range(max_attempts):
        raw = ask_llm(prompt)
        try:
            return parse_ticket(raw)
        except ValidationError as e:
            # galti model ko wapas batao aur ek baar aur try karo
            prompt = f"Your last answer was invalid:\n{e}\nReturn valid JSON only."
    raise ValueError("could not get valid output")


# Test with fake LLM: pehle broken, phir sahi
answers = iter(['not json', '{"category": "billing", "priority": 2}'])
print(get_ticket(lambda p: next(answers)))

Validation ke baad bhi cap lagao: retry sirf 1 se 2 baar. Warna ek hi galat output par paisa jalta rahega.

Flow: ek call kaise bachti hai

request
   |
   v
[timeout laga ke] primary model ---- ok ----> validate output ---- ok ----> user
   |  fail (timeout/429/5xx)                      | invalid
   v                                              v
retry with backoff (limited)               retry once with error message
   | still failing                                | still invalid
   v                                              v
fallback model -------------------------> graceful message / simple experience

Production reality

  • Retries ka cap aur total time budget. Har layer retry kare (client, gateway, service) to ek failure 3 x 3 x 3 = 27 calls ban sakta hai. Retry sirf ek ya do jagah karo.
  • Circuit breaker: agar provider baar-baar fail ho raha hai, kuch der ke liye usse call karna band karo (seedha fallback par jao) aur thodi der baad ek test call karo. Isse request latakti nahi.
  • 429 aur Retry-After ka respect karo (Chapter 8).
  • Streaming mein beech ka failure: stream shuru hone ke baad error aaye to status code badla nahi ja sakta. Error ko stream ke andar event ke roop mein bhejna padta hai, aur client ko "yahan se wapas try karo" ka tareeka do.
  • Agent loops par hard cap (max steps, max time, max cost). Ye fault tolerance ka hissa hai.
  • Chaos testing (halka sa): provider ko jaanbujhkar fail karwa ke dekho ki tumhara app kya karta hai. Tests mein fake LLM jo fail kare ya galat output de.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: timeout + 2 se 3 retries with backoff + validation + ek saaf error message. Phir fallback model.
  • Production-scale: circuit breakers, multi-provider failover gateway mein, degradation modes feature-wise, aur failure drills.

Trade-offs

  • Zyada retries: success badhta hai, par latency aur load badhta hai.
  • Fallback model: availability badhti hai, par quality aur cost alag ho sakte hain.
  • Validation retry: output theek hota hai, par extra cost aur latency.

Common galtiyan

  • Timeout hi nahi lagana.
  • Bina backoff ke turant retry (provider ko aur dabana).
  • Har error par retry (400 par bhi).
  • Side-effect wale tools par blind retry.
  • Har layer mein retry (retry storm).
  • LLM ke JSON par bharosa karna bina validation ke.
  • Fallback ko test hi na karna.

Connection

  • Chapter 8: 429 ka matlab hai "ruko", retry backoff se karo.
  • Chapter 9: gateway mein fallback aur retry ek jagah.
  • Chapter 5: streaming mein errors alag tarah se sambhalne padte hain.
  • Phase 1 project: llm.py mein timeout, retry, aur chat_json mein validation yahin se aati hai. Tests mein fake LLM se failures simulate karo.
  • Phase 3 mein agent ke tools (side effects) par retry aur idempotency ka dhyan.

Remember

Provider ka outage tumhare haath mein nahi, par app ka crash hai. Timeout lagao, backoff ke saath retry karo, fallback rakho, aur LLM ke output ko untrusted input ki tarah validate karo.

Test Yourself

  1. Story mein "turant 3 baar retry" kyun problem tha? Behtar kya hota?
  2. Kin errors par retry karna chahiye aur kin par nahi? Ek example do.
  3. "Email bhejo" tool timeout hua. Retry karna khatarnak kyun hai?
  4. LLM ne broken JSON diya. Tum kya karoge, aur kab haar maan lena chahiye?
  5. Teen layers (client, gateway, service) teeno 3 retry karein to ek failure par kitni calls ho sakti hain?

Phase 1 ebook yahan khatam

Ab project par jao: ../01-project-dockerized-agent.md. Wahan tum ye saari cheezein (streaming, cache, rate limit, timeout/retry, validation, cost logging) khud code karoge.

Chapter 11: Embeddings aur Vector Databases

Phase 2 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 25 minutes | Pehle Chapter 7 (semantic cache ka embedding wala hissa) padh lena

Story: "paise wapas" aur "refund" ek doosre ko nahi pehchante

Ek e-commerce company ke support docs mein ek page hai: "Refund Policy: order cancel hone par amount 5-7 din mein wapas aata hai."

Ek customer search box mein likhta hai: "mera paisa kab wapas milega?" Search kuch nahi deta. Doosra customer likhta hai "return karna hai", phir bhi kuch nahi.

Search engine ne wahi kiya jo use sikhaya gaya tha: words match karo. Page par "refund" likha hai, customer ne "paisa wapas" likha. Ek bhi word common nahi, to match zero.

Problem: keyword search meaning nahi samajhta

Keyword search (SQL LIKE, ya purane search engines) ke saath ye cheezein toot-ti hain:

  • Synonyms: "refund" vs "paisa wapas" vs "money back".
  • Alag tarah ka sawal: "kab tak milega" vs "processing time".
  • Spelling aur language ka mix (Hinglish users ke liye roz ki baat).

Hume aisa tareeka chahiye jo words ke peeche ka matlab compare kare.

Solution: embeddings

Embedding: text ko numbers ki ek lambi list (vector) mein badalna, is tarah ki jinka matlab similar ho, unke numbers paas-paas hon.

Ek embedding model (ek chhota neural network, LLM se alag cheez) text leta hai aur vector deta hai, jaise 768 ya 1536 numbers ki list. Dimension model par depend karta hai, apne model ka doc dekho.

Chapter 7 mein tumne semantic cache mein yahi dekha tha: "capital of France" aur "France ki capital?" ke vectors paas aate hain. Ab wahi idea documents search karne ke liye use ho raha hai.

Mental model: nakshe par points

Socho ek bahut bada nakshe (map) hai. Har sentence us nakshe par ek point hai. "Refund kab milega" aur "paisa wapas" ek hi mohalle mein baithte hain. "Office ka WiFi password" kisi aur sheher mein.

Search = "mera sawal nakshe par kahan girta hai? Uske sabse paas ke points kaun se hain?"

Asli nakshe 2D nahi hota, 768+ dimension ka hota hai, par idea wahi: doori = matlab ka farq.

Similarity kaise napte hain

Do vectors kitne "paas" hain, iske liye aksar cosine similarity use hoti hai.

Cosine similarity: do vectors ke beech ka angle napna. Same direction mein point karte hain to score 1 ke paas, bilkul alag direction mein to 0 ke paas (ya negative).

Formula: dot(a, b) / (norm(a) * norm(b)). Dot product bhi common hai; agar vectors pehle se normalized hain (length 1) to dono ka ranking same aati hai. Apne embedding model ka doc batata hai ki kaunsa metric chahiye.

Chhota demo: fake vectors, pure Python

Asli model ke numbers nahi, sirf samajhne ke liye 4 numbers wale fake vectors (jaise dimensions "paise", "delivery", "login", "product" ka bhaav maan lo):

import math

# Fake 4-dim vectors. Real models mein 768+ numbers hote hain.
DOCS = {
    "refund-policy": [0.9, 0.1, 0.0, 0.1],
    "delivery-times": [0.1, 0.9, 0.0, 0.2],
    "reset-password": [0.0, 0.0, 0.9, 0.1],
    "product-warranty": [0.3, 0.1, 0.0, 0.9],
}


def cosine(a, b):
    dot = sum(x * y for x, y in zip(a, b))
    norm_a = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in a))
    norm_b = math.sqrt(sum(y * y for y in b))
    return dot / (norm_a * norm_b)


def nearest(query_vec, docs, k=2):
    # Brute force: har doc se compare karo. Ise "flat" search kehte hain.
    scored = [(name, cosine(query_vec, vec)) for name, vec in docs.items()]
    scored.sort(key=lambda pair: pair[1], reverse=True)
    return scored[:k]


# "mera paisa kab wapas milega?" ka fake vector: paise wala hissa zyada
query = [0.8, 0.3, 0.0, 0.1]
for name, score in nearest(query, DOCS):
    print(f"{name}: {score:.3f}")

# Expected: refund-policy sabse upar (score 0.97 ke aaspaas),
# phir delivery-times (kam score).

Dhyan do: query mein "refund" word kahin nahi tha, phir bhi refund-policy upar aayi, kyunki vectors paas the. Yahi keyword search se farq hai.

Vector database ka kaam

Upar ka demo har document se compare karta hai. 4 documents ke liye theek hai. Par 10 million chunks hon to har query par 10 million comparisons bahut slow ho jaayenge.

Vector database: ek aisa database jo vectors (aur unke saath original text aur metadata) store karta hai aur "mere vector ke sabse paas wale k vectors do" query tezi se jawab deta hai.

Examples: Qdrant, Weaviate, Pinecone, Chroma. Kuch normal databases mein bhi vector support hai (PostgreSQL ka pgvector, OpenSearch). Phase 2 project mein tum Qdrant use karoge.

Index types (concept level)

Tezi ke liye vector DB ANN (Approximate Nearest Neighbor) use karta hai: thoda accuracy chhod kar bahut tez ho jao. Teen naam yaad rakho:

IndexIdeaKab
FlatHar vector se compare (exact)Chhota data (hazaaron chunks), ya accuracy sabse upar
HNSWPoints ka graph banao, shuru se paas ki taraf chalte jaoAksar default choice; index RAM mein fit ho
IVFVectors ko clusters mein baant do, sirf paas ke clusters dhundoMemory tight ho; aksar compression ke saath

Guide ke hisaab se aur bhi options hain (DiskANN, ScaNN) bahut bade scale ke liye; unki zarurat pehle nahi padti.

Honest baat: 5,000 se 50,000 chunks ke liye flat search bhi theek chal sakta hai. Pehle simple rakho, index tab sochna jab data aur latency problem ban jaaye.

Flow: indexing aur query

INDEXING (ek baar, offline)
 documents -> chunks -> embedding model -> [vector + text + metadata] -> vector DB

QUERY (har sawal par)
 user question -> SAME embedding model -> query vector
                                              |
                                              v
                        vector DB: top-k nearest chunks (cosine)
                                              |
                                              v
                                  chunks -> prompt -> LLM

Bahut zaroori: chunks aur query dono ke liye same embedding model hona chahiye. Alag models ke "nakshe" alag hote hain, unke vectors aapas mein compare nahi ho sakte.

Embedding model kaise chunein
