Technique 1: Quantization

Quantization: model ke numbers ko kam bits mein store karna (jaise 16-bit float se 8-bit ya 4-bit integer), taaki model chhota aur tez ho.

Socho ek photo ko 4K se compress karke WhatsApp par bhejna. File chhoti, thodi quality kam, par pehchaan mein aa jaati hai.

Chhota calculation (pure arithmetic): ek model mein 7 billion weights hain. 16-bit (2 bytes) mein 14 GB. 4-bit (0.5 byte) mein lagbhag 3.5 GB. Guide ka example bhi yehi kehta hai ki 70B model 16-bit mein lagbhag 140 GB aur 4-bit mein lagbhag 35 GB. Ye approximate numbers hain, real files mein thoda extra overhead hota hai.

Decode memory-bound hai, to chhote weights ka matlab har token par kam data move karna. Isliye quantization size aur speed dono deta hai, quality mein thoda sa nuksaan.

Neeche ek tiny illustration: floats ko int8 mein badlo aur wapas lao, error dekho.

import random

random.seed(7)
weights = [random.gauss(0, 0.5) for _ in range(8)]


def quantize_int8(values):
    scale = max(abs(v) for v in values) / 127   # poore group ke liye ek scale
    q = [round(v / scale) for v in values]       # ints in -127..127
    return q, scale


def dequantize(q, scale):
    return [x * scale for x in q]


q, scale = quantize_int8(weights)
back = dequantize(q, scale)
for w, i, b in zip(weights, q, back):
    print(f"{w:+.4f} -> {i:+4d} -> {b:+.4f}  err={abs(w - b):.5f}")
print("max error:", max(abs(w - b) for w, b in zip(weights, back)))
print("bytes: float32 =", 4 * len(weights), " int8 =", len(weights))

Ye run karne par max error lagbhag 0.002 aata hai aur storage 4 guna kam. Dhyan do:

  • Har weight thoda sa galat ho gaya (rounding). Ye hi quality ka "nuksaan" hai.
  • Agar group mein ek bahut bada outlier ho, scale bada ho jaata hai aur baaki sab numbers ka error badh jaata hai. Real quantization methods (AWQ, GPTQ, GGUF jaise naam, docs check karo) isi problem ko chhote groups aur smart tricks se handle karte hain.
  • 4-bit par error aur zyada hota hai (sirf 16 levels), isliye quality testing zaroori hai.

Production tip: quantized model ko apni eval set par chalao (Phase 2 ke evals). "Benchmark mein theek tha" tumhare task par theek ho, ye zaroori nahi.

Technique 2: Continuous batching

Chapter 4 mein dekha ki ek request akele GPU ko poora use nahi kar paati. Isliye server kai requests ko batch mein saath chalata hai.

Static batching: 4 requests ka batch ek saath shuru, aur jab tak sabse lamba jawab khatam na ho, naya batch shuru nahi hota.

Continuous batching: har step par dekhte hain ki koi request khatam hui? Agar haan, uski jagah turant waiting wali request daal do. GPU ka batch hamesha bhara rehta hai.

Analogy: static batching = bus jo tab tak nahi chalegi jab tak sab sawari utar na jaye. Continuous batching = metro, jahan har station par koi utarta hai aur koi chadhta hai.

Static (poora batch sabse lambe ka intezaar karta hai)
  Step 1: [ R1  R2  R3  R4 ]
  Step 3: [ R1  R2  --  R4 ]   R3 ki jagah khali, waste
  Step 5: [ --  --  --  R4 ]   sirf R4 chal rahi hai
  Step 6: [ R5  R6  R7  R8 ]   ab naya batch

Continuous
  Step 2: [ R1  R2  R3  R4 ]   R3 khatam
  Step 3: [ R1  R2  R5  R4 ]   R5 turant andar
  Step 4: [ R1  R6  R5  R4 ]   R2 khatam, R6 andar

Ek tiny timing simulation. Har number ek request ke output tokens hain, aur ek step mein har running request ka ek token banta hai:

def static_batching(lengths, slots):
    t = 0
    finish = []
    for i in range(0, len(lengths), slots):
        batch = lengths[i:i + slots]
        t += max(batch)            # poora batch sabse lambe tak chalta hai
        finish += [t] * len(batch)
    return finish


def continuous_batching(lengths, slots):
    waiting = list(lengths)
    running = []                   # har running request ke bache hue steps
    finish = []
    t = 0
    while waiting or running:
        while waiting and len(running) < slots:
            running.append(waiting.pop(0))   # khali slot turant bharo
        t += 1
        running = [r - 1 for r in running]
        finish += [t] * sum(1 for r in running if r == 0)
        running = [r for r in running if r > 0]
    return finish


lengths = [5, 50, 8, 6, 40, 7, 9, 45]   # output tokens per request
for name, fn in [("static", static_batching), ("continuous", continuous_batching)]:
    f = fn(lengths, slots=4)
    print(f"{name:10s} total steps={max(f):3d}  avg finish={sum(f) / len(f):.1f}")

Is toy example mein static ke total 95 steps aur continuous ke 58 steps aate hain, aur average finish time 72.5 vs 25.2. Ye sirf simulation hai (real GPU mein prefill, memory aur scheduling bhi hote hain), par idea saaf dikhta hai: chhote jawab lambe jawab ke peeche nahi atkte, aur GPU idle nahi rehta.

Guide ise inference optimization ki sabse badi jeet kehta hai. vLLM jaise engines ye default mein karte hain. Tumhe self-host karte waqt ye on mila hoga, bas samajhna hai ki ye kyun hai.

Technique 3: Speculative decoding

Decode ek-ek token karke chalta hai, aur har token par bade model ko poora weights padhna padta hai. Slow.

Idea: ek chhota fast draft model agle 4 tokens ka andaza lagata hai. Phir bada model un 4 tokens ko ek hi pass mein verify karta hai. Jo sahi, rakho. Jahan galat, bade model ka sahi token lo.

Intern ne email ka draft likha, manager ek nazar mein check kar deta hai. Manager ko poora email zero se likhne se kam time lagta hai.

Ye kyun chalta hai: decode memory-bound hai, to 4 tokens ko ek saath check karna lagbhag utna hi time leta hai jitna 1 token banana. Sahi acceptance rule ke saath output wahi rehta hai jo bade model akele deta (guide ka claim, aur ye is technique ka standard point hai).

Draft (chhota, tez):  "The cat sat on"
Big model verify (1 pass):  The OK | cat OK | sat NO -> "ran"
Result: "The cat ran"   (3 tokens, 1 bade pass mein)

Fayda tab zyada jab GPU mein spare compute ho (kam ya medium load). Bahut zyada load par fayda ghat jaata hai. Guide ke hisaab se 2x-3x tak speed-up milta hai, par ye workload par depend karta hai.

Technique 4: Test-time compute aur reasoning models

Pehle model smart banane ka ek hi tareeka tha: bada karo, zyada train karo. Ab doosra lever hai: jawab dete waqt zyada sochne do.

Test-time compute: model ko answer dene se pehle bahut saare "thinking" tokens likhne dena (ya kai attempts chalana), taaki mushkil problems par quality badhe.

Reasoning models yehi karte hain. Ye hidden thinking tokens banate hain, jo output tokens ki tarah bill hote hain (Chapter 1: reasoning tokens ek alag category). Guide ke hisaab se ek query par 10x se 100x tak zyada tokens ban sakte hain; exact number model aur task par depend karta hai.

Teen patterns:

  • Sequential: ek lambi soch ki chain.
  • Parallel: kai baar chalao, best chuno ya vote lo (jab answer verify ho sakta ho, jaise tests wala code).
  • Hybrid: dono.

Isse system design par asar:

  • Cost badhta hai lagbhag token count ke saath. Per-request token budget lagao.
  • Latency badhti hai (kai second se minutes). UI mein "thinking" state dikhao (Chapter 5, streaming).
  • Batching mushkil: 50,000 token ki reasoning request ek 200 token wali chat ke saath mix karna throughput bigaadta hai. Kai teams alag pools rakhti hain.
  • Router lagao (Chapter 7): sab queries reasoning model par mat bhejo. Easy ko sasta model.

Aksar ki galti: "reasoning model sab jagah better hai." Nahi. Simple extraction ya FAQ answer ke liye ye mehnga aur slow overkill hai.

Technique 5: Flash Attention (sirf idea)

Attention ka math wahi rehta hai, par standard tareeke mein ek bahut bada N x N score matrix GPU ki badi-par-slow memory (HBM) mein likha aur padha jaata hai. GPU ka math nahi, data ka aana-jaana slow hai.

Flash Attention kehta hai: matrix ko chhote blocks mein tod kar GPU ki chhoti-par-tez on-chip memory (SRAM) mein hi nipta lo, poora matrix HBM mein kabhi likho hi mat.

Analogy: library (HBM) bahut door hai, desk (SRAM) saamne. Har line ke liye library mat jao, ek baar kitaab laao, desk par kaam nipta ke wapas jao.

Result wahi, speed zyada. Quality ka koi nuksaan nahi, kyunki ye same attention ka smarter computation hai. Guide ke hisaab se lambi sequences par 2x-4x tak tez. Tumhe ise on nahi karna padta, modern engines aur frameworks andar use karte hain.

Technique 6: Mixture of Experts (MoE)

Ek hospital mein har patient sabhi doctors se nahi milta. Receptionist sahi specialist ke paas bhejta hai.

Mixture of Experts: model ke andar kai chhote "expert" networks aur ek router hota hai, jo har token ke liye sirf kuch (aksar 2) experts chunta hai. Baaki idle.

Token -> Router -> [E3] [E5]  (chune gaye)   E1 E2 E4 E6 E7 E8 (idle)
                     \  /
                  weighted sum -> output

System design ke liye iska matlab:

  • Memory total parameters ke hisaab se chahiye (saare experts load rehte hain).
  • Compute per token sirf active parameters ke hisaab se. Isliye "bada model, par tez" mil sakta hai.
  • Guide ka example: Mixtral 8x7B mein lagbhag 47B total aur lagbhag 13B active (approximate).

Matlab MoE se token bahut sasta milta hai, par GPU memory ka bill kam nahi hota. Ye subtle point interview mein achha lagta hai.

Technique 7: Distillation

Distillation: ek bade "teacher" model ke jawab dekh kar ek chhote "student" model ko unki nakal karna sikhana.

Ye quantization se alag hai. Quantization wahi model chhota karta hai. Distillation naya, chhota model banata hai.

Kab sochna: high-volume aur narrow task (classification, extraction, summarization), jahan bade model ka per-token kharch bahut hai. Limit: student har cheez mein teacher jaisa nahi hota. Chhote, fixed task par aksar bahut paas pahunch jaata hai.

Tumhare liye practical roop: aksar tum pehle se distilled/chhota model choose karte ho, khud distill nahi karte. Fine-tuning (LoRA jaise) ka zikr guide mein alag hai, aur guide ka rule: fine-tuning aksar zaroori nahi hota, pehle prompting aur RAG.

Technique 8: Grouped Query Attention (GQA)

Chapter 6 mein KV cache ka size dekha tha: har head ka apna K aur V. Bahut heads, bahut cache.

Grouped Query Attention: kai query heads ek hi Key aur Value share karte hain. KV cache chhota, quality lagbhag same.

Analogy: 8 students har ek ka alag notebook (cache) rakhne ki jagah 2 group, har group ka ek shared notebook.

Asar: wahi GPU par zyada users ya zyada lambi context. Ye model ki architecture ka hissa hai, tum model choose karte waqt hi iska fayda lete ho.

Ek table: kaun si technique kis cheez par hamla karti hai

TechniqueKya kam/behtar karti haiPhase
QuantizationWeights ka size, decode ka data movementDecode (aur memory)
Continuous batchingGPU idle time, throughputDono
Speculative decodingDecode ka time per token (TPOT)Decode
Test-time computeQuality badhati hai, cost/latency badhti haiDecode (bahut tokens)
Flash AttentionAttention ka memory trafficMainly prefill, lambi context
Mixture of ExpertsCompute per token (memory nahi)Dono
DistillationModel hi chhotaDono
GQAKV cache ka sizeDecode
Prefix/prompt cache (Ch. 6)Prefill ka kaamPrefill, TTFT

Simple rule: pehla shabd late to prefill side dekho. Streaming slow ya users kam fit to decode aur memory side dekho.

Flow: ek self-hosted request par kya-kya lagta hai

Request
  |
  v
Engine (vLLM-type)  -- continuous batching, paged KV cache
  |
  v
Prefill  (Flash Attention, prefix cache)  -> TTFT
  |
  v
Decode loop (quantized weights, GQA cache, speculative decoding) -> TPOT
  |
  v
Streamed tokens (Chapter 5)

Production reality

  • Hosted API: provider ye sab pehle se karta hai. Tum sirf model choose karte ho, prompt cache ka order sahi rakhte ho (Chapter 6), aur reasoning budget set karte ho.
  • Self-hosted: tum engine, quantization level, max batch aur max context choose karte ho. Har change ke baad latency (TTFT, TPOT) aur quality eval dono napo.
  • Quality ka regression chup-chaap aata hai. 4-bit quantized model ya chhota student demo mein theek lagta hai, par tumhare 5% hard cases mein fail ho sakta hai. Eval set ke bina pata nahi chalega.
  • Load test karo. Speculative decoding jaisi cheez low load mein jeetti hai, high load mein kam.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple (pehle yehi): hosted API, sahi model size, prompt cache, streaming, max output tokens limit, reasoning sirf zaroorat par.
  • Production-scale: self-hosted engine, quantized ya distilled model, autoscaling, alag pools (chat vs reasoning), prefix-aware routing.

Self-host tab sochna jab volume itna ho ki API bill GPU bill se zyada ho, ya data bahar nahi ja sakta (Chapter 2 aur privacy wale chapters). Chhote scale par aksar API sasti padti hai.

Trade-offs

  • Quantization: memory aur speed, thoda quality loss.
  • Continuous batching: throughput, par individual latency load ke saath badal sakti hai.
  • Speculative decoding: speed, extra draft model aur complexity.
  • Reasoning: quality, par cost aur latency.
  • MoE: compute sasta, memory nahi.
  • Distillation: bahut sasta, par sirf narrow task par.

Common galtiyan

  • "Quantization se model bekaar ho jaata hai." Nahi, aksar chhota quality drop. Par napo, maan mat lo.
  • "Flash Attention approximation hai." Nahi, same result, smarter computation.
  • "MoE ka matlab kam GPU memory." Nahi, saare experts memory mein rehte hain.
  • "Reasoning model hamesha behtar." Simple tasks par bekaar kharch.
  • "Distillation aur quantization same hain." Pehla naya chhota model, doosra wahi model kam precision mein.
  • Application engineer se ye sab implement karne ki umeed rakhna. Zyadatar tum choose aur configure karte ho.

Interview vs Production

  • Interview mein: naam, ek line ka "kyun", kis phase ko target karta hai (prefill ya decode), aur trade-off. Jaise "continuous batching kyun? Static batch sabse lambe request ka intezaar karta hai."
  • Production mein: kaun sa knob tumhare haath mein hai (model choice, quantization level, max tokens, batch config), aur uske baad eval aur latency measurement.

Connection

  • Chapter 4: prefill compute-bound, decode memory-bound. Ye chapter usi ke upar ke fixes hain.
  • Chapter 6: KV cache aur prompt cache. GQA aur paged attention cache ka size/waste kam karte hain.
  • Chapter 2: inference engines aur hardware. Chapter 3: TTFT, TPOT, throughput, jinhe ye techniques badalti hain.
  • Chapter 7: routing se reasoning model ka kharch control hota hai.
  • Chapter 28: system design interview mein "model selection" step mein ye trade-offs kaam aate hain.

Remember

Decode memory se bandha hai, isliye har inference trick ya to data chhota karti hai (quantization, GQA, distillation), ya GPU ko bhara rakhti hai (batching), ya ek pass mein zyada kaam karati hai (speculative decoding); aur reasoning model quality ke liye paisa aur time kharchta hai.

Test Yourself

  1. Decode memory-bound hai, to quantization speed kyun badhata hai? Quality ka nuksaan kahan se aata hai?
  2. Static batching mein chhota jawab lambe jawab ke peeche kyun atkta hai, aur continuous batching ise kaise theek karta hai?
  3. MoE model ko total parameters ke hisaab se memory aur active parameters ke hisaab se compute kyun lagta hai?
  4. Tumhare product mein 90% queries simple FAQ hain. Reasoning model ko kaise use karoge, aur cost kaise control karoge?
  5. Distillation aur quantization mein kya farq hai? Kab kaun sa sochoge?

Chapter 28: How to Solve Any AI System Design Problem (8-step framework)

Phase 5 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 40 minutes | Pehle Chapters 1-27 ka rough idea ho to best; ye chapter unhe jodta hai

Story: interviewer ne bola "Design a support chatbot"

Rahul ne 6 mahine RAG, agents, MCP sab padha. Interview mein sawal aaya: "Design a customer support chatbot for a SaaS company."

Rahul turant bola: "Hum vector DB lenge, Kubernetes par deploy karenge, multi-agent banayenge..." Interviewer ne beech mein roka: "Kitne users? Kya budget? Bot kya karega jab jawab na pata ho?"

Rahul ke paas koi jawab nahi tha. Usne tools ka naam liya, par problem ko samjha hi nahi.

Doosri candidate Meera ne pehle 5 minute sirf sawal puche, phir ek simple design diya, phir bataya ki kab complex banayegi. Offer Meera ko gaya.

Farq knowledge ka nahi tha. Farq ek order ka tha: pehle samjho, phir design karo.

Problem: building blocks bahut hain, order nahi

Ab tak tumne 27 chapters mein bahut blocks seekhe: caching, routing, RAG, agents, guardrails, observability. Interview mein sab ek saath yaad aate hain, aur banda sab ek design mein thoons deta hai.

Do galtiyan:

  • Tools se shuru karna (problem se nahi).
  • Bina bataye complex design (over-engineering).

Solution: 8 steps ka framework

Guide ke hisaab se har AI system design problem ke liye ye 8 steps follow karo:

  1. Requirements
  2. AI Objective
  3. Data Preparation
  4. Architecture Design
  5. Model Selection and Prompting
  6. Evaluation
  7. Deployment and Serving
  8. Monitoring

Isko ek dusre frame mein bhi dekho jo har system design ka base hai:

Requirements -> Constraints -> Design -> Trade-offs

Pehle 3 steps requirements aur constraints hain. Steps 4-5 design. Steps 6-8 ye proof dene ke liye ki design kaam karta hai aur kaam karta rahega. Trade-offs har step mein bolte chalo.

Mental model: dukaan kholne se pehle ka plan

Dukaan kholne se pehle: kaun customer, kitne, kya bechna, budget kitna (requirements). Phir kya cheez chahiye (data). Phir layout (architecture). Phir staff (model). Phir trial run (evaluation). Phir opening (deployment). Phir roz ka hisaab (monitoring).

Koi bhi dukaan pehle furniture khareed kar plan nahi karta.

Flow

1 Requirements -> 2 AI Objective -> 3 Data
                                      |
                                      v
8 Monitoring <- 7 Deploy <- 6 Evaluate <- 5 Model + Prompt <- 4 Architecture
      |
      +--> (kuch toota? wapas step 3-6, eval dobara chalao)

Simple-first rule (sabse zaroori)

Sabse simple design se shuru karo jo kaam kare. Complexity tab jodo jab requirement ya measurement force kare.

Pehle ek single LLM call. Phir zarurat padi to RAG. Phir zarurat padi to reranker, cache, router. Agent sabse aakhir mein, aur sirf tab jab steps pehle se pata na hon (Chapters 15-18 ka agents wala hissa).

Interview mein ye line bolna bahut achha lagta hai: "Pehle main V1 simple rakhunga, aur ye 3 signals dikhein to ye component jodunga." Isse dikhta hai ki tum production soch rakhte ho.

Har step mein kya karna hai

Step 1: Requirements

Sawal pucho, aur jawab likho:

  • Users aur scale: kitne users, kitni chats per day, kitne messages per chat.
  • Latency: pehla token kitne time mein (Chapter 3).
  • Budget: per month.
  • Data: kya data hai, kitna, kitna fresh.
  • Actions: sirf jawab dena ya kaam bhi karna (tools).
  • Failure behavior: jawab na pata ho to kya (escalate to human).
  • Safety/privacy: PII, multi-tenant, compliance.

Functional requirements (kya kare) aur non-functional (kitna tez, sasta, safe) alag bolo.

Step 2: AI Objective

Ek line mein: input kya, output kya, aur kaun sa pattern.

Jaise "user ka question + history -> help center par based grounded answer, ya escalate signal". Ye private data par Q&A hai, to pattern RAG hai (Chapters 11-14).

Pattern chunne ka quick map:

  • Private knowledge par Q&A -> RAG.
  • Fixed steps wala kaam -> workflow/orchestration.
  • Open-ended, steps pehle se nahi pata -> agent.
  • Naya behavior/format, bahut data -> fine-tuning (aksar zaroori nahi).

Step 3: Data Preparation

  • Kaun sa data source of truth hai, use clean karo (purane articles hatao, HTML/navigation hatao).
  • PII hatao jahan data eval ya training mein jaa raha ho.
  • Eval set abhi banao (hand-picked questions + expected answers). Ye aage har change ko napega.

Step 4: Architecture Design

Yahan diagram banao (Chapters 5-10 aur 11-26 ke blocks). Har component ke saath ek "kyun" bolo, aur kuch cheezein jaan-boojh kar chhodo.

Step 5: Model Selection and Prompting

  • Sasta aur fast model pehle, mushkil queries par bada model (routing, Chapter 7).
  • Embedding model aur reranker bhi yahin choose hote hain.
  • System prompt ke rules: "sirf context se jawab do, na pata ho to bolo na pata hai".
  • Prompt cache ke liye fixed hissa upar (Chapter 6).
  • Inference ke trade-offs (quantization, reasoning cost) Chapter 27 se.

Step 6: Evaluation

  • Eval set par chalao: answer quality, faithfulness (kya context par based hai), refusal accuracy (jab nahi pata to mana kiya?), retrieval quality.
  • LLM-as-judge ki limits yaad rakho; thoda human review bhi.
  • Har prompt/model/chunking change ke baad dobara chalao.

Step 7: Deployment and Serving

  • Hosted API ya self-host (aksar chhote scale par API).
  • Streaming (Chapter 5), rate limits (Chapter 8), gateway (Chapter 9), fallback aur timeouts (Chapter 10).
  • Guardrails aur privacy (Chapters 22 aur 23).
  • Canary rollout: pehle 10% users.

Step 8: Monitoring

  • Latency: TTFT, total time.
  • Cost: per user, per feature, per day.
  • Quality: thumbs up/down, escalation rate, sampled faithfulness.
  • Errors, cache hit rate, drift (users ke sawal ya docs badal gaye?).
  • Alerts: cost spike, latency, quality drop.

Worked example: customer-support RAG bot

Problem: SaaS company ke liye support chatbot. (Guide ke numbers example ke taur par; tum interview mein apne numbers puchhna.)

1. Requirements (interviewer se jawab maan lo):

  • Sawal: product usage, billing, account. Sirf English.
  • Scale: 100k users, 50k chats/day, lagbhag 5 messages/chat.
  • First token 1 second se kam. Budget limited.
  • 500 help articles aur purani chats available. Sirf jawab dena, actions nahi.
  • Pata na ho to human ko escalate.

2. AI Objective: question + history -> grounded answer ya escalate. Pattern: RAG.

3. Data: 500 articles clean karo; purani chats se PII hatao aur unse 200 sawal ka eval set banao.

4. Architecture:

User -> Backend -> (embed query) -> Hybrid search (vector + keyword)
                                          |
                                          v
                                  Reranker (top 50 -> top 5)
                                          |
                                          v
                        LLM (system prompt + chunks + question)
                                          |
                                          v
                                   Streamed answer (SSE)
        side: prompt cache, semantic cache, rate limit, guardrails

Simple-first: 500 articles ke liye aksar Postgres + pgvector ya chhota vector store kaafi hota hai; dedicated vector DB zaroori nahi.

5. Model + Prompt: sasta model default, "I do not know" par bade model ko ek chance ya seedha escalate. "Sirf context use karo" rule.

6. Evaluation: 200-question eval, faithfulness, refusal accuracy, retrieval hit rate (Phase 2 project jaisa).

7. Deployment: hosted model API, per-user limits, SSE ke liye load balancer ka lamba timeout, fallback model, input/output guardrails, canary 10%.

8. Monitoring: TTFT, cost/day, thumbs-up rate, escalation rate, cache hit rate, faithfulness sampling. Alerts in sab par.

Trade-offs bolo: reranker latency badhata hai par quality; semantic cache sasta par galat match ka risk; escalation kam karne se hallucination badh sakta hai.

Phase 2 ka RAG project isi ka chhota roop hai. Phase 5 ke practice prompts mein isi framework se jawab likho.

Chhoti estimation: back-of-envelope

Interview mein numbers ka rough idea dikhao. Ye snippet sirf placeholder prices use karta hai (apne provider ki current price se badlo, docs check karo):

chats_per_day = 50_000
msgs_per_chat = 5
input_tokens_per_msg = 2_500    # system prompt + 5 chunks + history (assumed)
output_tokens_per_msg = 250     # assumed

# PLACEHOLDER prices per 1M tokens, not real quotes
price_in_per_m = 0.50
price_out_per_m = 2.00

msgs = chats_per_day * msgs_per_chat
in_tok = msgs * input_tokens_per_msg
out_tok = msgs * output_tokens_per_msg

cost_day = in_tok / 1e6 * price_in_per_m + out_tok / 1e6 * price_out_per_m
peak_rps = msgs / 86_400 * 3        # assume peak is 3x average

print("messages/day:", msgs)
print("input tokens/day (M):", in_tok / 1e6)
print("cost/day (placeholder):", round(cost_day, 2))
print("cost/month (placeholder):", round(cost_day * 30, 2))
print("peak requests/sec (3x avg):", round(peak_rps, 1))

Seekh: input tokens aksar output se bahut zyada hote hain, to prompt cache (Chapter 6) aur kam chunks ka seedha asar bill par. Numbers galat ho sakte hain, par tarika dikhana zaroori hai: messages -> tokens -> cost aur requests/sec.

Production reality

  • Interviewer ko aksar perfect design nahi chahiye. Use soch ka process dikhna chahiye.
  • 45 minute ka interview: lagbhag 5-8 min requirements, 5 min objective+data, 15 min architecture+model, 10 min eval+deploy+monitor, baaki trade-offs aur sawal. (Ye ek suggested rough split hai.)
  • Real systems mein compromise hote hain. Ye bolna theek hai: "V1 mein ye nahi karunga, kyunki..."

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: single LLM call + prompt + chhota eval + basic logging.
  • Production-scale: RAG pipeline, routing, caches, gateway, guardrails, tracing, canary, alerts.

Trade-offs (har problem mein baar-baar aane wale)

Taraf ATaraf B
QualityCost aur latency
Fresh dataCache ka fayda
Zyada guardrailsZyada latency aur false refusals
Agent ki flexibilityWorkflow ki predictability
Self-host controlAPI ka kam operational kaam

Ek page ka checklist (whiteboard par ye table banao)

StepPucho / BoloTypical output
1 RequirementsUsers, scale, latency, budget, data, actions, failure, safetyLikhi hui requirements list
2 AI ObjectiveInput, output, pattern (RAG / workflow / agent)Ek line ka objective
3 DataSource, cleaning, PII, eval setData plan + eval set
4 ArchitectureComponents, request flow, kya chhodaDiagram
5 Model + PromptModel size, routing, prompt rules, cacheModel + prompt choices
6 EvaluationQuality, faithfulness, refusal, retrieval, regressionEval plan
7 DeploymentAPI/self-host, streaming, limits, fallback, guardrails, canaryServing plan
8 MonitoringLatency, cost, quality, errors, drift, alertsDashboard + alerts

Har row ke saath ek trade-off bolo, aur aakhir mein "pehle V1 simple, phir ye upgrade".

Common galtiyan

  • Requirements pooche bina diagram banana.
  • Har problem mein agent ya multi-agent daalna.
  • Eval aur monitoring bhool jaana (AI mein ye hi sabse zyada fark banata hai).
  • Cost aur latency ke numbers na dena.
  • Security/privacy ko last minute mein ek line mein nipta dena, jab multi-tenant ya PII ho.
  • Tool names ratna, par "kyun" na bata paana.

Connection

  • Chapters 1-4: tokens, cost, latency, prefill/decode ke numbers estimation mein.
  • Chapters 5-10: streaming, caching, routing, rate limit, gateway, fault tolerance deployment step mein.
  • Chapters 11-26: RAG, agents, tools, MCP, memory, safety, privacy, observability, cost, scaling: architecture aur monitoring ke blocks.
  • Chapter 27: model selection ke trade-offs.
  • Chapter 29: 3 real case studies isi framework se todkar dekhte hain.
  • Phase 5: 4 system design prompts isi framework par paper par solve karo.

Remember

Pehle problem samjho, phir simple design do, phir eval aur monitoring se saabit karo: Requirements, Objective, Data, Architecture, Model, Evaluation, Deployment, Monitoring.

Test Yourself

  1. 8 steps ko order mein likho aur bolo ki sabse pehla kyun requirements hai, architecture nahi.
  2. "Simple-first rule" kya hai? Support bot mein tum pehle V1 mein kya rakhoge aur baad mein kya jodoge?
  3. Support bot ke liye 3 requirement sawal batao jinka jawab design badal dega.
  4. Ek chat mein 2,500 input aur 250 output tokens hain. 50,000 chats/day par daily token aur cost estimate kaise nikaaloge?
  5. Evaluation step ko kaun si 3 cheezein napni chahiye, aur kab dobara chalani chahiye?

Chapter 29: Real-World AI System Case Studies

Phase 5 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 35 minutes | Pehle Chapter 28 (framework) padh lena

Story: "Claude Code ya Cursor kaise kaam karta hai?"

Interview mein poocha gaya: "Tumne Cursor use kiya hai? Andar se kaise kaam karta hoga?"

Ek candidate bola: "Wo bahut smart AI hai." Doosre ne bola: "Codebase ko chunks mein todke embeddings banata hoga, retrieval se relevant code nikalta hoga, bade model ko bhejta hoga, aur edit ko file mein apply karta hoga."

Doosra jawab sahi ho ya na ho, uski soch alag hai: wo product ko pehle se seekhe blocks mein tod raha hai. Ye chapter wahi skill sikhata hai.

Ek zaroori disclaimer

Ye chapter in products ka guide ka description aur ek simplification hai. Mujhe in companies ke asli internal code ya architecture ka pata nahi. Concepts achhe hain aur interview mein kaam aate hain, par "Cursor internally exactly ye karta hai" jaisa claim mat karo. Bolo: "Publicly jo described hai uske hisaab se aur ek reasonable design ye hoga."

Tumhara asli kaam: kisi bhi product ko blocks mein decompose karna.

Decomposition ka tareeka

Har case ke liye 4 sawal poocho:

  1. User kya chahta hai (objective)?
  2. Kaun sa data ya context chahiye?
  3. Kaun se building blocks (loop, retrieval, tools, streaming, memory...) lagte hain?
  4. Latency, cost, safety ka kya constraint hai?

Ye Chapter 28 ke framework ka mini roop hai.

Case 1: Coding agent (Claude Code jaisa)

Kya karta hai: terminal mein plain English task do. Wo code padhta hai, files edit karta hai, commands chalata hai, aur apna kaam check karta hai.

Central idea: agent loop

Agent loop: LLM ek step sochta hai, ek action leta hai, result dekhta hai, aur ye tab tak repeat hota hai jab tak kaam poora na ho.

Guide ise 3 phases mein dikhata hai: gather context, take action, verify results.

Your request
     |
     v
+--> Gather context  (files search/read, project notes)
|         |
|         v
|    Take action     (edit file, run command)
|         |
|         v
|    Verify results  (run tests / program)
|         |
+-- not done
          | done
          v
     Task complete

Blocks jo isme dikhte hain

BlockYahan kaise
Agent loop + harnessHarness (LLM ke aas-paas ka code) loop chalata hai, tools call karta hai, context sambhalta hai
Tool callingSearch, read, edit, run-command tools: LLM ki "aankhein aur haath". LLM sirf tool maangta hai, tool tumhara code chalata hai
VerificationTests chalana. Passing test = machine-checkable "done"
Memory fileProject ki notes ki ek file (jaise CLAUDE.md) har session ke start mein context mein load hoti hai
GuardrailsPermissions: risky action (command chalana, file delete) se pehle user ki approval
SubagentsBadi search alag agent ko di jaati hai, taaki main context saaf rahe
Context compactionLambi session ko summary mein badalna, taaki agent ghanton chal sake
Skills aur MCPSkills = kaam karne ka tareeka, MCP = bahari tools se connection

Samajhne wali baatein

  • Verify step sabse important: LLM galat code likh sakta hai. Test chalake khud pakadna agent ko reliable banata hai. Isiliye achhe tests wala repo agents ke saath behtar kaam karta hai.
  • Context sabse mehnga resource: har file padhna context bharta hai. Isiliye subagents aur compaction hain.
  • Safety: agent ke paas haath hain (commands). Least privilege aur approval gates zaroori (prompt injection wale chapters).
  • Cost: har loop iteration ek LLM call hai. Loop cap aur token budget lagao.

Ek tiny, illustrative loop (stub LLM, runnable):

def fake_llm(history):
    """Stub: pehle test chalao, fail dikhe to fix, phir done."""
    last = history[-1]
    if last == "start":
        return {"tool": "run_tests"}
    if last == "tests failed":
        return {"tool": "edit_file"}
    if last == "file edited":
        return {"tool": "run_tests"}
    return {"done": True}


def run_tool(name, state):
    if name == "run_tests":
        return "tests passed" if state["fixed"] else "tests failed"
    if name == "edit_file":
        state["fixed"] = True
        return "file edited"


state = {"fixed": False}
history = ["start"]
for step in range(5):                     # loop cap
    action = fake_llm(history)
    if action.get("done") or history[-1] == "tests passed":
        print("done after", step, "steps")
        break
    result = run_tool(action["tool"], state)
    history.append(result)
    print(step, action["tool"], "->", result)

Ye asli product ka code nahi, sirf shape dikhata hai: act, observe, verify, repeat, with a cap.

Case 2: AI code editor (Cursor jaisa), concept level

Kya karta hai: ek code editor jisme AI hai: autocomplete, codebase se sawal-jawab, aur file mein change apply karna.

Block 1: codebase par RAG

Chapters 11-14 ke RAG ka seedha use. Guide ke description ke hisaab se:

  • Code ko chunks mein todo (jaise ek function ek chunk).
  • Har chunk ka embedding banao aur vector database mein rakho.
  • User poochhe "password kahan check hota hai?" to meaning se search karke matching code LLM ko bhejo.
Index time:  code files -> chunks -> embeddings -> vector store
Query time:  question -> embedding -> top matching chunks -> LLM -> answer/edit

Dhyan rahe: code ke liye sirf meaning-search aksar kaafi nahi hota. Exact naam (function ka naam) ke liye keyword search bhi kaam aata hai, to hybrid sochna (RAG chapters ka hybrid search). Ye mera suggested design hai, product ke internals ka claim nahi.

Index ko fresh rakhna ek real problem hai: file badli to uske chunks dobara index karne padte hain (sirf badli hui files, poora repo nahi).

Block 2: model routing by job (Chapter 7)

Guide ka table (concept level):

KaamKaisa modelKyun
Tab autocompleteChhota, fastTyping ke saath instant dikhna chahiye
Chat / agentBada, smartMushkil sochna aur code likhna
Change apply karnaChhota, fastDescribe kiya gaya change file mein merge karna chhota kaam hai

Har model ka ek clear kaam. Bada model sochta hai, chhote models tez kaam nipta dete hain. Product smart bhi aur fast bhi lagta hai.

Block 3: edit application

Edit application: bade model ka output aksar "ye badlav karo" ke roop mein hota hai, poori file nahi. Use asli file mein sahi jagah par lagana ek alag step hai.

Simplification: ek tarika ye hai ki LLM sirf badla hua hissa likhe, aur alag (aksar chhota) model ya code usse file mein merge kare. Guide "Apply model" ka zikr karta hai; exact tareeka products ke hisaab se alag ho sakta hai. Safety ke liye: apply ke baad diff dikhao, user approve kare, aur syntax/test check chalao.

Samajhne wali baatein

  • Latency alag-alag budget: autocomplete ko millisecond-level chahiye, agent task kai second le sakta hai.
  • Privacy: code company ki property hai. Kya embeddings bahar jaate hain, kya data train mein use hota hai, ye enterprise ke liye bada sawal hota hai (privacy chapters).
  • Context selection: kaun si files LLM ko dikhani hain, ye quality ka bada hissa hai (context management chapters).

Case 3: Real-time voice AI agent

Kya karta hai: phone ya app par support call. User bolta hai, agent bolkar jawab deta hai, aur zarurat par tools (order lookup) use karta hai.

Pipeline: STT -> LLM -> TTS

STT (speech-to-text): awaaz ko text mein badalna. TTS (text-to-speech): text ko awaaz mein badalna.

User ki awaaz -> [STT] -> text -> [LLM + tools] -> text -> [TTS] -> awaaz

Streaming har jagah

Agar har stage poora khatam hone ka intezaar kare, to user ko kai second ki chuppi milti hai. Isliye har stage stream hoti hai: STT partial text deta hai, LLM tokens stream karta hai (Chapter 5), TTS pehle sentence ko bolna shuru kar deta hai, jabki LLM agla sentence abhi likh raha hota hai. Stages overlap hote hain.

Latency budget

Latency budget: poori baat-cheet ke total "jawab dene mein kitna time" ko stages mein baant kar har stage ka limit tay karna.

Insaani baat-cheet mein 1 second ke aas-paas ka gap bhi "slow" lagne lagta hai (ye ek general rule of thumb hai, exact number product par depend karta hai). Isliye ek sample budget (illustrative, real numbers nahi):

StageExample budget
Turn khatam hone ka detection (user ruka?)200 ms
STT ka final text100 ms
LLM ka pehla token (TTFT, Chapter 3)300 ms
TTS ka pehla audio150 ms
Network100 ms
Total850 ms

Isi liye voice agent mein aksar chhota aur fast LLM use hota hai, prompt cache (Chapter 6) aur kam context. Reasoning model yahan mushkil hai, kyunki wo seconds leta hai (Chapter 27).

Barge-in aur turn detection

  • Turn detection: system ko pata chalna ki user ne bolna khatam kiya ya sirf saans li. Galat hua to agent beech mein bol padega ya bahut der chup rahega.
  • Barge-in: user agent ko beech mein toke. Agent ko turant TTS rokna aur audio buffer khali karna hota hai, aur LLM ka baaki output fenk dena hota hai. Ye streaming ka sabse tricky hissa hai.

Scale

Guide ka estimate: 10 lakh calls/day, har 5 minute ki, to lagbhag 12 calls/second, aur ek time par kai hazaar calls chal rahe hote hain. Har call ko STT, LLM, TTS ki streaming session chahiye, to cost mostly GPU/model ka hota hai. Isliye har jagah chhote fast models. (Ye calculation aap khud kar sakte ho: 1,000,000 calls x 300 seconds / 86,400 seconds = lagbhag 3,500 average concurrent calls; peak mein 3x maan lo.)

Samajhne wali baatein

  • Failure: STT galat sun le to LLM galat jawab dega. Fallback: "Kya aap dohra sakte hain?" aur human handoff.
  • Safety/privacy: call recordings mein PII hota hai (privacy chapters). Tool calls par guardrails (kisi ka refund bina verification ke nahi).
  • Observability: per-stage latency napo, kyunki "slow" ka source STT, LLM ya TTS kuch bhi ho sakta hai (Chapter 3 aur observability chapters).

Mapping table: kaun sa case kaun se chapters use karta hai

BlockCase 1: Coding agentCase 2: Code editorCase 3: Voice agentChapter
Tokens, costHaanHaanHaan1
Latency (TTFT, TPOT)ThodaAutocomplete mein bahutBahut3, 4
StreamingHaanHaanCore5
KV / prompt cacheHaan (lambe session)HaanHaan (TTFT)6
Model routingKabhi-kabhiCoreFast model choose7
Fault tolerance, fallbackHaanHaanHaan (human handoff)10
RAG / embeddingsContext gather meinCoreFAQ lookups mein11-14
Context managementCore (compaction)CoreChhota context15-18

MCP, memory, multi-agentMemory file, subagents, MCPKuchKam19-22
Safety, guardrails, approvalsCore (permissions)Apply approvalCore19-22
PrivacyCode ka dataCode ka dataCall recordings23
ObservabilityTracesHaanPer-stage latency24
Cost, scalingLoop capChhote modelsGPU concurrency25-26
Inference trade-offsKamChhote/bade modelsChhota fast model27
8-step frameworkPooraPooraPoora28

Production reality

  • Teeno cases mein latency, cost aur safety ka ek-doosre se takraav hai. Verification aur guardrails latency badhate hain; agent loops cost badhate hain; fast models quality ghatate hain.
  • Real products mein bahut sa glue code, retries, queues aur monitoring hota hai jo blog diagrams mein nahi dikhta. Diagram simple, asliyat messy.
  • In products ko "poora khud bana lo" ek bada project hai. Tum interview mein skeleton aur trade-offs batate ho, aur apne chhote project (Phase 1-4) mein ek-do blocks sach mein bana ke dikhate ho.

Simple vs production-scale

  • Simple: coding agent ka toy version: ek LLM, 3 tools (read, edit, run tests), loop cap 10, approval prompt. Ek hafte-end ka project.
  • Production-scale: sandboxing, subagents, compaction, permissions, observability, cost caps. Voice mein: media gateway, GPU pools, telephony.

Common galtiyan

  • Product ka internal exact architecture claim karna (jo tumhe pata nahi).
  • Sirf "AI karta hai" bolna, blocks na batana.
  • Coding agent mein verify step bhool jaana.
  • Voice agent mein har stage ko sequential samajhna (streaming overlap na batana).
  • Code editor mein sirf vector search bolna, exact-match search aur index freshness bhool jaana.

Connection

  • Chapter 28: har case isi 8-step framework se todo.
  • Chapters 5, 6, 7, 10: streaming, caches, routing, fallback teeno cases mein.
  • Chapters 11-26: RAG, agents, tools, safety, privacy, observability.
  • Chapter 27: voice aur editor mein chhote fast models kyun.
  • Phase 5: practice prompt 2 (coding assistant jo repository mein files edit karta hai) ka seedha reference Case 1 aur Case 2 hain.

Remember

Koi bhi bada AI product ek jaadu nahi, seekhe hue blocks ka jodh hai: agent loop aur verify (coding agent), retrieval aur routing (code editor), streaming aur latency budget (voice agent).

Test Yourself

  1. Coding agent ke loop ke teen phases kaun se hain, aur verify phase ke bina kya hoga?
  2. Code editor mein codebase ko chunk aur embed kyun karte hain? Sirf meaning-search ki kya limit ho sakti hai?
  3. Voice agent ke latency budget mein kaun se stages hain, aur reasoning model use karna kyun mushkil hai?
  4. Barge-in kya hai aur use handle karne ke liye system ko kya karna padta hai?
  5. Kisi bhi case ko leke batao ki kaun se 4 chapters ke blocks use hue aur ek trade-off kya hai.

Chapter 30: Interview Question Bank aur Glossary

Phase 5 | Ye chapter padhne ka time: about 45 minutes (padhna) + practice alag | Pehle Chapters 1-29 ek baar dekh lena

Story: interview se ek raat pehle

Neha ke paas kal AI Engineer ka interview hai. Usne 29 chapters padhe hain, par raat ko dar lag raha hai: "Kya mujhe sab yaad hai? Aur bolna kaise hai?"

Usne ek kaam kiya: har topic ke liye ek sawal uthaya aur 2 minute mein khud ko zor se jawab diya. Jahan atki, wahi chapter dobara khola.

Ye chapter tumhare liye wahi kaam ready-made hai: sawal, jawab ka rough outline, aur kahan padhna hai. Ye model answer ka outline hai, ratne wala script nahi. Apne words aur apne project ke example ke saath bolo.

Jawab dene ka simple format

Har sawal par ye 4 cheezein bolo:

  1. Ek line ki definition ya seedha jawab.
  2. Kyun (kaun si problem solve karta hai).
  3. Ek example (apne project se ho to best).
  4. Trade-off ya limit (kab fail hota hai).

Aur phir ek diagram paper par. Phase 5 ki tarah: 2 minute mein explain, diagram ke saath.

Interview Question Bank

Neeche har jawab ka outline chhota hai. Poore jawab ke liye bataya gaya chapter padho.

A. LLM basics aur serving (Chapters 1-10)

Q1. Tokens kya hain aur cost token se kyun judta hai? Token text ka chhota tukda hai, model words nahi tokens padhta hai. Bill input aur output tokens par banta hai, aur output aksar mehnga hota hai. Isliye prompt chhota rakhna aur output limit lagana cost control ka pehla kadam hai. (Chapter 1)

Q2. TTFT, TPOT aur throughput mein kya farq hai? TTFT = pehla token aane mein time, TPOT = baad ke har token ka time, throughput = system ek second mein kitna kaam nipta raha hai. User ko pehla shabd jaldi chahiye, system owner ko zyada throughput. Dono kabhi-kabhi ek doosre ke khilaaf jaate hain. (Chapter 3)

Q3. Prefill aur decode kya hain, aur ye alag kyun hain? Prefill poora prompt ek saath padhta hai (compute-bound, TTFT decide karta hai). Decode ek-ek token likhta hai (memory-bound, TPOT decide karta hai). Isi wajah se lamba prompt shuruaat slow karta hai aur lamba output streaming time badhata hai. (Chapter 4)

Q4. KV cache aur prompt cache mein kya farq hai? Prompt cache hit rate kab girta hai? KV cache ek request ke andar pichhle tokens ka K,V yaad rakhta hai. Prompt cache alag requests ke beech same shuruaat (prefix) reuse karta hai. System prompt mein timestamp jaisi badalne wali cheez upar daalne se hit rate zero ho jaata hai. (Chapter 6)

Q5. Streaming kyun zaroori hai, aur SSE kya hai? LLM ka poora jawab banne mein seconds lagte hain, to user ko tokens aate hi dikhao. SSE ek simple one-way HTTP stream hai server se browser tak. Dhyan: load balancer ka timeout aur buffering. (Chapter 5)

B. Production: cost, reliability, gateway (Chapters 7-10, 25-26)

Q6. LLM application ka cost aur latency kaise kam karoge? Pehle measure karo (kahan zyada token ja rahe). Phir sasta model easy queries par (routing), prompt cache, semantic cache, chhota output, kam aur behtar chunks, streaming. Har step ka trade-off bolo (quality ya staleness). (Chapters 6, 7, 25)

Q7. Semantic cache kya hai aur uska risk kya hai? Wo exact words nahi, meaning se milte sawal ka purana jawab de deta hai. Risk: galat match ya multi-tenant data leak. Isliye similarity threshold aur per-tenant isolation. (Chapter 7)

Q8. Provider down ho ya slow ho to kya karoge? Timeouts, limited retries with backoff, fallback model ya provider, graceful degradation, aur output validation. Retries bina limit ke bhaari galti hai. Ye sab gateway par ek jagah rakhna aasaan hota hai. (Chapters 9, 10)

Q9. Rate limiting AI mein alag kyun hai? Sirf requests per minute nahi, tokens per minute, concurrency aur cost bhi limit karna padta hai, kyunki ek request kuch tokens se lekar lakhon tokens tak ho sakti hai. Per-user aur per-tenant limits. (Chapter 8)

C. RAG (Chapters 11-14)

Q10. RAG pipeline end to end samjhao. Chunking mein trade-off kya hai? Documents clean karo, chunks mein todo, embeddings banake store karo; query par top chunks nikalo, prompt mein daalo, LLM context se jawab de. Chhote chunks precise par context kam; bade chunks context zyada par noise aur cost. Overlap thoda rakhte hain. (Chapters 11-14)

Q11. Hybrid search aur reranking kyun? Vector search meaning pakadta hai par exact naam/code miss kar sakta hai; keyword search exact pakadta hai. Dono milao. Reranker top-N ko dobara order karke best top-k deta hai. Cost: thodi extra latency. (Chapters 11-14)

Q12. RAG ko evaluate kaise karoge? Hallucinated citations kaise rokoge? Retrieval alag napo (sahi chunk top-k mein aaya?) aur generation alag (jawab context par based hai?). Faithfulness check, refusal accuracy, chhota hand-made eval set. Citations ke liye chunk IDs prompt mein do aur output mein verify karo ki ID sach mein retrieved set mein hai. (Chapters 11-14, aur evaluation ka hissa)

D. Context aur agents (Chapters 15-18)

Q13. Context window bhar jaye to kya karoge? Truncation, summarization/compaction, sliding window, ya sirf relevant chunks retrieve karna. Lambi context free nahi: cost, latency aur "lost in the middle" ka risk. (Chapters 15-18)

Q14. Agent kya hai? Kab agent use nahi karoge? LLM + instructions + tools + memory + loop. Jab steps pehle se pata hon, fixed workflow behtar: sasta, predictable, debug karna aasaan. Agent tab jab steps run-time par decide hone hon. Loop cap aur token budget zaroor. (Chapters 15-18, 28, 29)

Q15. Tool calling ka round trip samjhao. LLM sirf bolta hai "ye tool is argument se chalao"; tumhara code tool chalata hai, result wapas LLM ko deta hai, LLM aage sochta hai. Argument validation aur structured output zaroori. Tool kabhi LLM khud nahi chalata. (Chapters 15-18)

E. MCP, memory, multi-agent, safety (Chapters 19-22)

Q16. MCP kya hai aur function calling se kaise alag hai? MCP ek open standard hai jisse AI apps ek hi tarike se tools aur data se jud sakein. Function calling model ki ek ability hai (tool maangna); MCP is baat ka standard hai ki tools kaise describe aur serve hon, taaki har app ke liye alag integration na likhna pade. Security: untrusted server aur least privilege. (Chapters 19-22)

Q17. Multi-agent kab sahi hai? Jab kaam alag specialized parts mein bantta ho ya context saaf rakhna ho (subagents). Cost, latency aur debugging badhte hain, to pehle single-agent baseline se compare karo. (Chapters 19-22, aur Phase 3 project)

Q18. Prompt injection kya hai aur kaise bachoge? Attacker ka likha text model ko tumhari instruction ki jagah apni instruction follow karwa deta hai (document, email, web page ke andar chhupa ho sakta hai). Maan lo model hijack ho sakta hai: tools par least privilege, hard rules code mein, irreversible action par human approval, untrusted data aur tools wale model ko alag rakhna, logging. Prompt mein "ignore attacks" likhna kaafi nahi. (Chapters 19-22)

F. Privacy, observability, evals (Chapters 23-24)

Q19. Multi-tenant AI app mein ek tenant ka data doosre ko leak na ho, ye kaise sunishchit karoge? Retrieval par tenant filter (data layer par, prompt mein nahi), per-tenant cache keys, alag quotas, logs mein PII redact, aur tests jo cross-tenant access try karein. (Chapters 7, 23-26)

Q20. Production mein AI system ko observe kaise karoge? Eval kyun zaroori hai? Traces (har LLM call, tool call, retrieval ek span), token/cost/latency, quality signals (thumbs, escalation). Eval set prompt ya model badalne par regression pakadta hai; bina eval ke "better hua ya nahi" andaza hi hai. (Chapter 24, 28)

G. Optimization aur system design (Chapters 27-29)

Q21. Quantization, continuous batching, speculative decoding: ek-ek line mein. Quantization = kam bits mein weights, chhota aur tez model, thoda quality loss. Continuous batching = khatam hui request ki jagah turant nayi, GPU idle nahi. Speculative decoding = chhota model guess kare, bada ek pass mein verify kare. Application engineer aksar inhe choose/configure karta hai, implement nahi. (Chapter 27)

Q22. AI system design problem ko kaise approach karoge? 8 steps: requirements, AI objective, data, architecture, model + prompt, evaluation, deployment, monitoring. Simple V1 se shuru, har component ka reason, aur trade-offs bolo. (Chapter 28)

Q23. Coding agent (jaise Claude Code) ya voice agent ko blocks mein tod ke samjhao. Coding agent: gather context, act, verify loop, tools, memory file, permissions. Voice agent: STT -> LLM -> TTS, streaming har jagah, latency budget, barge-in. Bolo ki ye concept-level description hai. (Chapter 29)

Red flags: jawab mein ye mat karo

  • Problem samjhe bina tools ke naam lena ("vector DB + Kubernetes + agents").
  • Har cheez ke liye agent ya multi-agent bolna, workflow ka option na batana.
  • Numbers aur trade-offs na dena (cost, latency, scale ka koi rough hisaab nahi).
  • Eval aur monitoring ko bhool jaana, jabki AI mein ye sabse bada farq banate hain.
  • Security ko last mein ek line mein nipta dena (prompt injection, tenant leak, PII).
  • Prompt mein likhna hi security hai bolna ("prompt mein bata denge ki leak mat karo").
  • Unverified claims: "mere system ne 99% accuracy di" jab measure hi nahi kiya. Interviewer har line par khodega.
  • Resume mein jo nahi banaya, wo bolna. Sirf wahi claim karo jo tumne build aur measure kiya (Phase 5 ka rule).
  • Product ke internals ka confident claim jab pata nahi ("Cursor exactly ye karta hai"). Bolo "publicly described / mera reasonable guess".
  • Definitions ratna, bina example ya "kyun" ke.
  • Retry ko free samajhna: bina cap ke retries outage badhate hain.
  • Naye model/tool ke naam par atakna: concepts par jao, naam aur prices badalte rehte hain.

Glossary (40 terms)

TermMatlab (ek line)
TokenText ka chhota tukda jo LLM padhta aur likhta hai
Context windowModel ek baar mein jitne tokens dekh sakta hai
InferenceTrained model se naye input par output nikalna
Inference serverGPU par model chalane aur requests serve karne wala server (vLLM jaisa)
TTFTTime to First Token: pehla token aane tak ka time
TPOTTime Per Output Token: baad ke har token ka time
ThroughputSystem ek second mein kitna kaam (tokens/requests) nipta raha hai
PrefillPoora prompt ek saath process karna (TTFT decide karta hai)
DecodeOutput tokens ek-ek karke banana (memory-bound)
KV cachePichhle tokens ke Key/Value ko yaad rakhna, taaki dobara na nikaalna pade
Prompt cacheAlag requests ke same prefix ka kaam reuse karna
Semantic cacheMeaning se milte sawal ka purana jawab dena
LLM routingHar query ko sahi (sasta/bada) model par bhejna
AI gatewaySaari LLM calls ka ek common entry point (limits, fallback, logging)
Streaming (SSE)Tokens ko banate hi user tak bhejna
Rate limitingRequests, tokens ya cost par limit lagana
EmbeddingText ke meaning ko numbers ki list mein badalna
Vector databaseEmbeddings store karke sabse milte jule jaldi dhoondhne wala database
ChunkBade document ka chhota tukda jo index hota hai
RAGJawab dene se pehle apne documents se relevant text nikaal kar LLM ko dena
Hybrid searchKeyword aur vector search ko milana
RerankerRetrieved results ko question ke hisaab se dobara order karne wala model
HallucinationModel ka confidently galat ya banaya hua jawab dena
FaithfulnessJawab kitna retrieved context par based hai
Context compactionPurani baat-cheet ko chhoti summary mein badalna
AI agentLLM + tools + memory + ek loop jo goal poora hone tak chalta hai
HarnessAgent loop chalane, tools call karne wala LLM ke aas-paas ka code
Tool / function callingLLM ka tumhare code se function chalwane ka request karna
Structured outputLLM se schema ke hisaab se clean JSON lena
MCPAI apps ko tools aur data se jodne ka open standard
SubagentMain agent ke neeche chhota specialized agent jo alag context mein kaam kare
GuardrailsLLM ke input/output par safety checks
Prompt injectionAttacker ka text model ko apni instruction follow karwana
Least privilegeAgent/tool ko sirf utni hi permission dena jitni zaroori hai
PIIPersonally identifiable information: naam, phone, email jaisa personal data
ObservabilitySystem ke andar kya hua, traces aur metrics se dekh paana
Eval setSawal + expected answers ka set jo system ki quality napta hai
LLM-as-a-judgeEk LLM se doosre LLM ke output ko score karwana
QuantizationWeights ko kam bits mein store karke model chhota aur tez banana
Continuous batchingKhatam hui request ki jagah turant nayi request batch mein daalna
DistillationBade teacher model ki nakal karke chhota student model banana

Self-quiz (10 sawal, bina answers ke)

  1. Prefill aur decode alag kyun hain, aur har ek kaun sa metric decide karta hai?
  2. Tumhare system prompt mein "Current time" sabse upar hai aur prompt cache kaam nahi kar raha. Kyun, aur fix kya hai?
  3. RAG bot galat jawab de raha hai. Retrieval problem hai ya generation problem, ye kaise alag karoge?
  4. Kaunsa kaam fixed workflow se karoge aur kaunsa agent se? Ek-ek example do.
  5. Prompt injection se bachne ke liye "prompt mein likh do" kyun kaafi nahi hai? Do achhe defenses batao.
  6. Ek tenant ka data doosre tenant ko retrieval ya cache ke through kaise leak ho sakta hai?
  7. Continuous batching static batching se kyun behtar hai? Ek chhota example do.
  8. Support chatbot ke liye 8-step framework ka har step ek line mein batao.
  9. Voice agent ka latency budget kin stages mein baanta jaata hai, aur barge-in kya hai?
  10. Resume ya interview mein ek claim jo tumne measure nahi kiya, use kaise handle karoge?

  • Outline ko ratna. Apne words aur apne project ke example se bolo.
  • Sirf padhna, bolna nahi. Zor se 2 minute bolo, aur paper par diagram banao.
  • Sirf easy topics dohrana. Jahan atke, wahi chapter dobara padho.

Connection

  • Chapters 1-10: LLM basics aur serving ke sawal (A, B).
  • Chapters 11-26: RAG, agents, MCP, safety, privacy, observability, cost, scaling (C-F).
